### Leveraging Reinforcement Learning, Genetic Algorithms and Transformers for background determination in particle physics
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14894v1)
- **Authors**: Guillermo Hijano Mendizabal, Davide Lancierini, Alex Marshall, Andrea Mauri, Patrick Haworth Owen, Mitesh Patel, Konstantinos Petridis, Shah Rukh Qasim, Nicola Serra, William Sutcliffe, Hanae Tilquin
- **Abstract**: Experimental studies of beauty hadron decays face significant challenges due to a wide range of backgrounds arising from the numerous possible decay channels with similar final states. For a particular signal decay, the process for ascertaining the most relevant background processes necessitates a detailed analysis of final state particles, potential misidentifications, and kinematic overlaps, which, due to computational limitations, is restricted to the simulation of only the most relevant backgrounds. Moreover, this process typically relies on the physicist's intuition and expertise, as no systematic method exists. This paper has two primary goals. First, from a particle physics perspective, we present a novel approach that utilises Reinforcement Learning (RL) to overcome the aforementioned challenges by systematically determining the critical backgrounds affecting beauty hadron decay measurements. While beauty hadron physics serves as the case study in this work, the proposed strategy is broadly adaptable to other types of particle physics measurements. Second, from a Machine Learning perspective, we introduce a novel algorithm which exploits the synergy between RL and Genetic Algorithms (GAs) for environments with highly sparse rewards and a large trajectory space. This strategy leverages GAs to efficiently explore the trajectory space and identify successful trajectories, which are used to guide the RL agent's training. Our method also incorporates a transformer architecture for the RL agent to handle token sequences representing decays.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a novel algorithm for automatically determining relevant background processes in particle physics experiments, focusing on beauty hadron decays. It addresses the current lack of systematic methods and the reliance on physicist expertise. The algorithm combines Reinforcement Learning (RL) and Genetic Algorithms (GAs) and incorporates a transformer architecture. The RL agent, trained on signal processes, learns to generate background decay sequences. GAs are used to explore the large trajectory space and identify successful trajectories to guide RL agent training. The transformer architecture allows the RL agent to handle token sequences representing decays. The authors adapt the AlphaZero algorithm and introduce a policy gradient approach with supervised updates (PGSU) and a periodic expert guidance (PEG) for agent training. A toy scenario with 40 particles is developed to evaluate the algorithm's performance.  Experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of the combined approach, showing the RL agent achieving beyond-expert performance with the help of GA-generated expert demonstrations. Finally, the learned embeddings produced by the transformer were visualized.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper's strength lies in addressing a practical problem in particle physics with a novel combination of machine learning techniques.  Currently, the background determination is a time-consuming and expert-dependent process, introducing potential biases and limiting scalability. The proposed algorithm offers a potentially more systematic and automated approach, potentially reducing human error and accelerating analysis.

**Novelty:**

*   **Combining RL, GAs, and Transformers:**  While RL and GAs have been individually applied to particle physics problems, the synergy presented is innovative. Using GAs to provide expert demonstrations addresses a critical challenge of sparse rewards in RL, a common issue in many particle physics applications. The use of transformers to model decay sequences appears natural and well-justified.
*   **Adaptation of AlphaZero and Implementation of PGSU/PEG:**  While the AlphaZero algorithm is not entirely new in machine learning, its application in the particle physics domain and particularly to this specific problem represents a significant novelty.  The introduction of PGSU and PEG as modifications addresses specific limitations of relying solely on GA-generated expert data.
*   **Addressing a Practical Problem:** The paper tackles a critical step in experimental particle physics, one that remains largely manual and subjective.

**Significance:**

*   **Potential Impact on Experimental Analyses:** If successfully deployed, the algorithm could significantly streamline and improve the accuracy of experimental particle physics analyses. Reduced systematic uncertainties in background determination could enhance the precision of Standard Model tests and searches for new physics.
*   **Applicability to Other Domains:** The RL-GA synergy could be applicable to other domains with sparse rewards and large trajectory spaces, such as drug discovery or materials science.
*   **Methodological Contribution:**  The PGSU and PEG modifications and the strategies for tokenizing decays offer new methodologies for applying RL in scientific contexts.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Toy Scenario:** The paper evaluates the algorithm on a "toy scenario." While useful for initial validation, it lacks the complexity and realism of actual experimental data. The 40-particle system and simplified approximations may not fully capture the challenges of real-world analyses with hundreds of potential backgrounds and complex detector effects.  The leap to a full-scale experimental analysis is a significant hurdle.
*   **Limited Evaluation Metrics:** The main evaluation metric is the number of correctly identified backgrounds. While useful, it doesn't fully assess the impact on downstream analyses. For example, does the algorithm improve the accuracy of parameter measurements or the sensitivity to new physics signals? A more thorough evaluation would involve simulating full experimental analyses with and without the algorithm.
*   **Lack of Consideration of Uncertainties:** The formulation focuses on *identifying* the background, without incorporating existing uncertainties of their cross-sections into the algorithm, which are critical in background estimation and eventually, systematic uncertainty estimation of any measurement.
*   **Scalability Concerns:** It is also unclear if the GA component could have a scalability issue for even larger background sets.
*   **Performance Trade-offs:** The analysis could also discuss the cost, time, and computer resources of the proposed method against current approaches.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a genuinely novel approach to a significant problem in particle physics. The combination of RL, GAs, and transformers is innovative, and the modifications made to the AlphaZero algorithm are well-motivated. If scalable to real experimental analyses, it could significantly improve the efficiency and accuracy of background determination. However, the evaluation on a toy scenario and the lack of comprehensive evaluation metrics limit the impact of the paper. A higher score would be justified with experiments on more realistic simulated or actual experimental data. While promising, the practical utility of this algorithm in a high-dimensional and complex setting is uncertain.

### CARGO: A Framework for Confidence-Aware Routing of Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14899v1)
- **Authors**: Amine Barrak, Yosr Fourati, Michael Olchawa, Emna Ksontini, Khalil Zoghlami
- **Abstract**: As large language models (LLMs) proliferate in scale, specialization, and latency profiles, the challenge of routing user prompts to the most appropriate model has become increasingly critical for balancing performance and cost. We introduce CARGO (Category-Aware Routing with Gap-based Optimization), a lightweight, confidence-aware framework for dynamic LLM selection. CARGO employs a single embedding-based regressor trained on LLM-judged pairwise comparisons to predict model performance, with an optional binary classifier invoked when predictions are uncertain. This two-stage design enables precise, cost-aware routing without the need for human-annotated supervision. To capture domain-specific behavior, CARGO also supports category-specific regressors trained across five task groups: mathematics, coding, reasoning, summarization, and creative writing. Evaluated on four competitive LLMs (GPT-4o, Claude 3.5 Sonnet, DeepSeek V3, and Perplexity Sonar), CARGO achieves a top-1 routing accuracy of 76.4% and win rates ranging from 72% to 89% against individual experts. These results demonstrate that confidence-guided, lightweight routing can achieve expert-level performance with minimal overhead, offering a practical solution for real-world, multi-model LLM deployments.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and a critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CARGO, a confidence-aware framework for routing user prompts to the most appropriate Large Language Model (LLM) in a multi-model deployment. CARGO uses an embedding-based regressor trained on LLM-judged pairwise comparisons to predict model performance, incorporating a binary classifier for uncertain predictions. It supports category-specific regressors across five task groups (mathematics, coding, reasoning, summarization, and creative writing). Evaluation shows CARGO achieves high routing accuracy and win rates against individual expert LLMs, demonstrating expert-level performance with minimal overhead.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Novelty in the Approach:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its reliance on LLM-judged pairwise comparisons for training the routing mechanism *without* human-annotated supervision. This is a significant advantage over traditional methods that require extensive labeled data, a bottleneck for scalability and adaptation.
    *   **Lightweight and Efficient:** The framework's reliance on an embedding-based regressor (as opposed to a large Transformer-based router) makes it lightweight and suitable for real-time deployments. The use of a binary classifier as a fallback mechanism in cases of uncertainty further optimizes the trade-off between accuracy and computational cost.
    *   **Task-Specific Specialization:** The inclusion of category-specific regressors allows CARGO to capture domain-specific LLM behavior, potentially leading to better routing performance.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper presents a thorough evaluation across multiple LLMs, diverse task categories, and a range of metrics, including routing accuracy, win rates, and computational cost. The ablation studies (adding a fifth expert and unseen domain) add more depth.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on LLMs as Judges:** While using LLMs to judge other LLMs is a clever way to avoid human labeling, it introduces a potential bias. If the judging LLMs are flawed or biased, the routing mechanism will inherit those flaws. The evaluation attempts to mitigate this with inter-judge agreement, self-preference bias analysis and human validation, it's not entirely removed.
    *   **Limited LLM Diversity:** The paper only evaluates four LLMs. The router's performance with a more diverse set of LLMs with vastly different architectures and capabilities remains to be seen.
    *   **Dataset and Domain Specificity:** The results are based on a specific curated dataset. While the dataset is diverse, the generalizability of CARGO to entirely new domains or tasks not represented in the training data is a concern. The ablation study only adds *one* unseen domain (translation), with a limited evaluation size.
    *   **Incremental Improvements:** While the results show that CARGO outperforms a straightforward approach, the gains achieved compared to strong ensemble methods might not always justify the additional complexity, and could be considered incremental.
    *   **Scalability Cost:** As shown by the provided study, the cost to label more models increases as `O(P*J*M)`, with `P` being prompts, `J` the judges and `M` the number of LLMs. While lower than human effort, it is not negligible and becomes a bottleneck with an increasing amount of LLMs.

*   **Significance:**

    The paper addresses a critical challenge in the LLM landscape: efficient and cost-effective routing of prompts to the best-suited model. The method is significant because it tackles the trade-off between performance, cost, and scalability in multi-model LLM deployments. By reducing reliance on human-annotated data and utilizing lightweight models, CARGO offers a practical solution for real-world applications. The confidence-aware approach is useful for reducing cost for certain applications, and is thus a step in the right direction.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a sound approach with promising results, but certain weaknesses need to be considered. The novelty is primarily in the efficient training mechanism with reduced human-labeled supervision. While LLM-based judging introduces a potential bias, this is addressed by thorough experiments to ensure it's aligned with human ratings. While a stronger analysis is performed on scaling on domains, this area could have benefited from more in-depth scalability analysis on more LLMs and domains, even if it could be included as future research. The improvements are good, and have the potential to bring better efficiency to existing systems. However, I believe the work is good, but not exceptional.

### A Comparative Evaluation of Large Language Models for Persian Sentiment Analysis and Emotion Detection in Social Media Texts
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14922v1)
- **Authors**: Kian Tohidi, Kia Dashtipour, Simone Rebora, Sevda Pourfaramarz
- **Abstract**: This study presents a comprehensive comparative evaluation of four state-of-the-art Large Language Models (LLMs)--Claude 3.7 Sonnet, DeepSeek-V3, Gemini 2.0 Flash, and GPT-4o--for sentiment analysis and emotion detection in Persian social media texts. Comparative analysis among LLMs has witnessed a significant rise in recent years, however, most of these analyses have been conducted on English language tasks, creating gaps in understanding cross-linguistic performance patterns. This research addresses these gaps through rigorous experimental design using balanced Persian datasets containing 900 texts for sentiment analysis (positive, negative, neutral) and 1,800 texts for emotion detection (anger, fear, happiness, hate, sadness, surprise). The main focus was to allow for a direct and fair comparison among different models, by using consistent prompts, uniform processing parameters, and by analyzing the performance metrics such as precision, recall, F1-scores, along with misclassification patterns. The results show that all models reach an acceptable level of performance, and a statistical comparison of the best three models indicates no significant differences among them. However, GPT-4o demonstrated a marginally higher raw accuracy value for both tasks, while Gemini 2.0 Flash proved to be the most cost-efficient. The findings indicate that the emotion detection task is more challenging for all models compared to the sentiment analysis task, and the misclassification patterns can represent some challenges in Persian language texts. These findings establish performance benchmarks for Persian NLP applications and offer practical guidance for model selection based on accuracy, efficiency, and cost considerations, while revealing cultural and linguistic challenges that require consideration in multilingual AI system deployment.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a comparative evaluation of four large language models (LLMs) – Claude 3.7 Sonnet, DeepSeek-V3, Gemini 2.0 Flash, and GPT-4o – for Persian sentiment analysis and emotion detection in social media texts. The study addresses a gap in cross-linguistic LLM performance understanding by focusing on Persian, a language with unique structural and cultural characteristics. The researchers used balanced Persian datasets, consistent prompts, and uniform processing parameters to ensure a fair comparison.  They analyzed performance metrics like precision, recall, and F1-scores, and also examined misclassification patterns. The results indicate acceptable performance levels across all models, with GPT-4o demonstrating slightly higher accuracy, while Gemini 2.0 Flash proved to be the most cost-efficient. The study reveals that emotion detection is more challenging than sentiment analysis for all models, and highlights cultural and linguistic challenges in Persian texts.  The paper aims to provide benchmarks for Persian NLP applications and practical guidance for model selection based on accuracy, efficiency, and cost.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The novelty lies in its focus on the Persian language, a relatively under-explored area in LLM evaluation compared to English.  While numerous papers evaluate LLMs, few systematically compare multiple models *specifically* on Persian sentiment and emotion tasks using rigorously controlled experiments. The detailed analysis of misclassification patterns offers valuable insights into the challenges posed by Persian language features.

* **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from addressing a critical gap in cross-linguistic LLM understanding.  By providing performance benchmarks and model selection guidelines tailored for Persian, the research contributes to the development of more effective and culturally sensitive NLP applications for Persian-speaking communities. The identification of linguistic and cultural challenges is also important for informing the design of future multilingual AI systems. The study results show the need for more evaluation of LLMs across different languages other than English because of the structural differences.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Rigorous Methodology:** The study employs a well-defined and reproducible methodology with balanced datasets, standardized prompts, and consistent processing parameters.
    * **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  The paper provides a detailed analysis of multiple performance metrics, including precision, recall, F1-score, and accuracy. The examination of misclassification patterns is particularly insightful.
    * **Practical Implications:** The study offers practical recommendations for model selection based on accuracy, efficiency, and cost, which are directly relevant to developers of Persian NLP applications.
    * **Publicly Available Resources:**  The authors make their code implementations and dataset processing steps publicly available, fostering transparency and reproducibility.
    * **Cultural and Linguistic awareness:** the paper identifies the unique structural differences of the Persian language that are not seen in English.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Scope of Datasets:**  The study relies on existing datasets that may be limited in size and representativeness. The datasets are social media based and are limited in scope as the paper stated. This could affect the generalizability of the findings.
    * **Zero-Shot Evaluation:** The LLMs were evaluated in a zero-shot setting, which might not fully reflect their performance potential if fine-tuned on Persian data. The fine-tuning of the models could result in better performance.
    * **Lack of Statistical Power:** While the paper uses statistical tests, the small dataset sizes may limit the statistical power to detect subtle differences between models. The paper states that the raw metric values hinted at rankings, but were not statistically significant.

* **Justification for Score:** While the paper is not groundbreaking in its general approach to LLM evaluation, its specific focus on Persian sentiment and emotion analysis, its rigorous methodology, and the practical insights it provides justify a score of **7**. The study is a solid and valuable contribution, advancing our understanding of LLM performance in a linguistically and culturally specific context. It's not a fundamentally transformative paper but it provides a crucial, well-executed analysis for a language that is often neglected in the broader NLP landscape. Further studies with larger, more diverse datasets and fine-tuned models could build upon these findings. The emphasis on replicable research and open sourcing data also allows for others to do more research on the subject and improve results.

### Cross-Modal Knowledge Distillation for Speech Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14930v1)
- **Authors**: Enzhi Wang, Qicheng Li, Zhiyuan Tang, Yuhang Jia
- **Abstract**: In this work, we present the first systematic evaluation of catastrophic forgetting and modality inequivalence in speech large language models, showing that introducing speech capabilities can degrade knowledge and reasoning even when inputs remain textual, and performance further decreases with spoken queries. To address these challenges, we propose a cross-modal knowledge distillation framework that leverages both text-to-text and speech-to-text channels to transfer knowledge from a text-based teacher model to a speech LLM. Extensive experiments on dialogue and audio understanding tasks validate the effectiveness of our approach in preserving textual knowledge, improving cross-modal alignment, and enhancing reasoning in speech-based interactions.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the issues of catastrophic forgetting and modality inequivalence in speech large language models (LLMs). It demonstrates that introducing speech capabilities can degrade a model's existing textual knowledge and reasoning abilities, especially when queries are spoken. To counter these problems, the authors propose a cross-modal knowledge distillation framework. This framework leverages both text-to-text and speech-to-text channels to transfer knowledge from a text-based teacher model to the speech LLM.  Experiments on dialogue and audio understanding tasks validate the approach's effectiveness in preserving textual knowledge, improving cross-modal alignment, and enhancing reasoning in speech interactions. The core idea is to treat the speech LLM as a "student" and the original text LLM as the "teacher," using distillation techniques to transfer knowledge in both text and speech domains.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of using knowledge distillation to improve speech LLMs is a valuable contribution. While individual components like knowledge distillation and speech LLMs aren't new, the application of cross-modal knowledge distillation (text-to-text and speech-to-text) to specifically address catastrophic forgetting *and* modality inequivalence in this context appears to be a novel combination. The systematic evaluation of these issues is also a valuable contribution. It stands out because it directly tackles dialogue task and the modality alignment aspect, differentiating it from works more focused on audio processing or instruction following. The paper argues well for the necessity of a targeted solution, based on observed performance declines under speech input that earlier methods using frozen backbones do not prevent.

*   **Significance:** The paper's findings are significant because they directly address practical challenges faced when extending text-based LLMs to the speech domain. Catastrophic forgetting and modality inequivalence are substantial hurdles to overcome to create truly useful speech-based interfaces. The proposed distillation framework offers a practical approach to mitigate these challenges, as supported by experimental results on standard benchmarks.
* **Clarity and Completeness:** The paper is well-written and clearly explains the problem, proposed solution, and experimental setup. The results are presented in a clear and understandable manner. One potential weakness is that the improvements, while present, are not overwhelmingly large (as evident in the tables). This leads one to consider how robust they might be under more difficult conditions, or whether a substantially larger training dataset might be required. However, the paper's findings are still of interest, and point towards a promising direction.
*   **Scope and Impact:** The work has the potential to influence future research in speech LLMs by highlighting the importance of addressing catastrophic forgetting and modality inequivalence and by providing a concrete approach to tackle these issues. While the scope is limited to a specific class of models (those that adapt text LLMs to speech), the impact could be broad given the increasing interest in voice interfaces and conversational AI. However, further research could investigate its scalability to more complex models and tasks.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Systematic Evaluation: Provides a thorough evaluation of catastrophic forgetting and modality inequivalence.
    *   Clear Problem Definition: Articulates the challenges clearly and concisely.
    *   Cross-Modal Knowledge Distillation: Presents a novel and effective approach.
    *   Experimental Validation: Supports the proposed method with experimental results.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Limited Improvements: The gains, while present, are not dramatically large, which raises questions about robustness and scalability.
    *   Model and Dataset Scale: Experiments are conducted on relatively smaller open-source models and limited datasets. Scaling the approach to larger models and datasets may reveal new challenges.
    * No ablation study is present. It would be useful to explore the impact of the 2 loss components (cross-entropy, Kullback Leibler divergence).

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of speech LLMs by addressing catastrophic forgetting and modality inequivalence. It offers a well-defined and experimentally validated solution using cross-modal knowledge distillation. While the improvements, though observable, are somewhat modest, and the work could benefit from further experimentation on larger models and datasets, the systematic evaluation and practical approach warrant a score of 7. The work lays a foundation for future research aiming to enhance the robustness and capabilities of speech LLMs.


- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Mitigating data replication in text-to-audio generative diffusion models through anti-memorization guidance
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14934v1)
- **Authors**: Francisco Messina, Francesca Ronchini, Luca Comanducci, Paolo Bestagini, Fabio Antonacci
- **Abstract**: A persistent challenge in generative audio models is data replication, where the model unintentionally generates parts of its training data during inference. In this work, we address this issue in text-to-audio diffusion models by exploring the use of anti-memorization strategies. We adopt Anti-Memorization Guidance (AMG), a technique that modifies the sampling process of pre-trained diffusion models to discourage memorization. Our study explores three types of guidance within AMG, each designed to reduce replication while preserving generation quality. We use Stable Audio Open as our backbone, leveraging its fully open-source architecture and training dataset. Our comprehensive experimental analysis suggests that AMG significantly mitigates memorization in diffusion-based text-to-audio generation without compromising audio fidelity or semantic alignment.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates mitigating data replication (memorization) in text-to-audio generative diffusion models using Anti-Memorization Guidance (AMG). It adapts the AMG framework, originally designed for image generation, to the audio domain. The approach involves steering the model away from memorized samples during inference using three guidance strategies: despecification guidance (reducing the influence of specific prompts), caption deduplication guidance (discouraging generation of samples with duplicated captions), and dissimilarity guidance (actively steering away from the nearest neighbor in the embedding space). The authors evaluate AMG on Stable Audio Open, a fully open-source text-to-audio model and dataset. The experiments demonstrate that AMG effectively reduces data replication without sacrificing audio quality or semantic alignment with the input text prompts.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its application of AMG to the audio domain, specifically for diffusion-based text-to-audio models. While AMG has been used in image generation, this is the first work, according to the authors, to address data replication in generative audio models using this approach. The adaptation and exploration of different guidance strategies within the AMG framework tailored to the audio domain contribute to the novelty. However, the core concept of AMG is not entirely new.

*   **Significance:** Data replication is a significant concern for generative models, especially in light of copyright and intellectual property issues. By demonstrating the effectiveness of AMG in mitigating memorization in audio models, the paper addresses a practical and pressing problem. The use of a fully open-source model and dataset is a strength, as it promotes reproducibility and transparency, allowing other researchers to build upon this work.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Addresses a relevant and timely problem in generative AI.
    *   Adapts a proven technique (AMG) to a new domain (audio).
    *   Provides a comprehensive experimental analysis with both qualitative and quantitative results.
    *   Uses a fully open-source model and dataset, promoting reproducibility and further research.
    *   Evaluates various guidance strategies and their combinations, providing insights into their individual and joint contributions.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The novelty is somewhat limited, as AMG itself is not a new technique. The paper primarily applies and adapts an existing method.
    *   The reliance on CLAP embeddings, while common, might limit the ability to capture subtle differences in audio quality.
    *   The paper lacks a detailed analysis of the computational overhead of AMG during inference.
    *   There's limited discussion on the sensitivity of the performance to hyperparameter tuning, especially for the guidance coefficients.
    *   While audio quality is measured, subjective listening tests could further strengthen the results.
    *   There is no direct comparision to other possible memorization mitigation strategies (e.g. pre-filtering of the dataset).

*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence the development of more ethically sound and responsible text-to-audio generative models. It provides a practical approach to mitigate data replication, which can help alleviate concerns about copyright infringement and intellectual property violations. The open-source nature of the work should encourage further research and development in this area.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution by adapting and applying AMG to the audio domain. It addresses a real problem and provides a practical solution with solid experimental evidence. However, the novelty is somewhat incremental since the core AMG framework is not entirely new. The analysis could be improved with more analysis of how hyperparmeters influence performance, a comparison to other memorization mitigation strategies, and subjective evaluations of the generated audio. Taking these considerations into account, I would assign it a score of:

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: eess.AS
- **Score**: 7/10

### A Comparative Analysis of Transformer Models in Social Bot Detection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14936v1)
- **Authors**: Rohan Veit, Michael Lones
- **Abstract**: Social media has become a key medium of communication in today's society. This realisation has led to many parties employing artificial users (or bots) to mislead others into believing untruths or acting in a beneficial manner to such parties. Sophisticated text generation tools, such as large language models, have further exacerbated this issue. This paper aims to compare the effectiveness of bot detection models based on encoder and decoder transformers. Pipelines are developed to evaluate the performance of these classifiers, revealing that encoder-based classifiers demonstrate greater accuracy and robustness. However, decoder-based models showed greater adaptability through task-specific alignment, suggesting more potential for generalisation across different use cases in addition to superior observa. These findings contribute to the ongoing effort to prevent digital environments being manipulated while protecting the integrity of online discussion.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've carefully reviewed the paper and will provide a concise summary, followed by a critical evaluation of its novelty and significance.

**Summary:**

The paper "A Comparative Analysis of Transformer Models in Social Bot Detection" investigates the effectiveness of bot detection models using encoder and decoder transformer architectures. The authors develop pipelines to evaluate the performance of these classifiers, finding that encoder-based classifiers generally exhibit higher accuracy and robustness. However, decoder-based models demonstrate greater adaptability through task-specific alignment (prompt engineering). The paper aims to contribute to preventing digital environments from manipulation while preserving the integrity of online discussion. The study also assesses how larger models, as well as feature sets, affect both architectures.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**

    While the application of transformer models to social bot detection is not entirely new, the paper's comparative analysis between encoder and decoder architectures is a valuable contribution. Previous research (cited in the paper) has explored transformers for bot detection, but this study specifically focuses on a direct comparison of the two primary architectures in different contexts and feature sets. Examining the task-specific tuning and the varying degrees with which model size affects the different architectures is a worthwhile undertaking.

*   **Significance:**

    The paper's findings have important implications for the development of more effective bot detection systems. The discovery that encoder-based models offer greater accuracy and robustness in the standard application could guide researchers and practitioners in choosing appropriate architectures for various bot detection scenarios. Furthermore, the insight that decoder-based models demonstrate greater adaptability through task-specific alignment (prompt engineering) provides a direction for future research into enhancing the performance of these models. The inclusion of information on model size and different feature sets also widens the overall scope.

    *   **Strengths:**

        *   Clear and well-defined research question.
        *   Rigorous methodology with well-defined pipelines for encoder and decoder models.
        *   Comparative analysis provides valuable insights into the strengths and weaknesses of different architectures.
        *   Considers various practical aspects, such as null response rates.
        *   Considers a range of experiments, including different feature sets, model sizes, and finetuning.
        * Provides well-defined information on all the experiments and how they relate.

    *   **Weaknesses:**

        *   The study is limited to a single dataset (TwiBot-22), which may affect the generalizability of the findings. The paper acknowledges this.
        *   The choice of only three open-source models of each architecture is limited, which also affects the generality of the research. Again, the paper acknowledges this.
        *   While the study considered enhanced and reduced feature sets, it did not utilize the full information contained in the TwiBot-22 dataset (e.g., user account relations).
        * Small sample size.

*   **Potential Impact:**

    The research has the potential to influence the design of bot detection systems, encouraging the use of encoder models for improved accuracy and robustness, while also suggesting methods for leveraging decoder models. Additionally, the study highlights the importance of task-specific tuning. The paper's findings could contribute to a safer and more trustworthy online environment by improving the detection of malicious bots.

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper presents a valuable comparative analysis of transformer architectures for social bot detection. While it has limitations related to generalizability due to the dataset and sample size used, the study's clear methodology, insightful findings, and potential impact justify a solid rating.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Explainable AI for Infection Prevention and Control: Modeling CPE Acquisition and Patient Outcomes in an Irish Hospital with Transformers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14942v1)
- **Authors**: Minh-Khoi Pham, Tai Tan Mai, Martin Crane, Rob Brennan, Marie E. Ward, Una Geary, Declan Byrne, Brian O Connell, Colm Bergin, Donncha Creagh, Nick McDonald, Marija Bezbradica
- **Abstract**: Carbapenemase-Producing Enterobacteriace poses a critical concern for infection prevention and control in hospitals. However, predictive modeling of previously highlighted CPE-associated risks such as readmission, mortality, and extended length of stay (LOS) remains underexplored, particularly with modern deep learning approaches. This study introduces an eXplainable AI modeling framework to investigate CPE impact on patient outcomes from Electronic Medical Records data of an Irish hospital. We analyzed an inpatient dataset from an Irish acute hospital, incorporating diagnostic codes, ward transitions, patient demographics, infection-related variables and contact network features. Several Transformer-based architectures were benchmarked alongside traditional machine learning models. Clinical outcomes were predicted, and XAI techniques were applied to interpret model decisions. Our framework successfully demonstrated the utility of Transformer-based models, with TabTransformer consistently outperforming baselines across multiple clinical prediction tasks, especially for CPE acquisition (AUROC and sensitivity). We found infection-related features, including historical hospital exposure, admission context, and network centrality measures, to be highly influential in predicting patient outcomes and CPE acquisition risk. Explainability analyses revealed that features like "Area of Residence", "Admission Ward" and prior admissions are key risk factors. Network variables like "Ward PageRank" also ranked highly, reflecting the potential value of structural exposure information. This study presents a robust and explainable AI framework for analyzing complex EMR data to identify key risk factors and predict CPE-related outcomes. Our findings underscore the superior performance of the Transformer models and highlight the importance of diverse clinical and network features.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores the application of Explainable AI (XAI) techniques to model and predict Carbapenemase-Producing Enterobacteriaceae (CPE) acquisition and patient outcomes using Electronic Medical Records (EMR) data from an Irish hospital. It compares various machine learning models, including Transformer-based architectures, for predicting 30-day readmission, in-hospital mortality, length of stay (LOS), and CPE acquisition risk. The study incorporates contact network features derived from patient ward co-location to model potential transmission pathways. XAI methods like Integrated Gradients are used to interpret model decisions and identify key risk factors, aiming to inform infection prevention and control (IPC) strategies.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Practical Application:** The paper addresses a significant real-world problem in healthcare - the spread of CPE infections, showcasing the potential of AI to aid in infection control.
    *   **XAI Integration:** It emphasizes explainability, a crucial aspect of AI adoption in clinical settings, using techniques like Integrated Gradients to understand model predictions. This increases trust and enables actionable insights.
    *   **Comprehensive Modeling:** It considers a range of factors, including patient demographics, clinical codes, contact network features, and past medical history.
    *   **Transformer Evaluation:** The comparative analysis of Transformer-based models with traditional methods provides valuable insights into their performance in healthcare prediction tasks.
    *   **Actionable Insights:** The findings identify potentially modifiable risk factors, such as admission ward and contact patterns, that can inform targeted IPC interventions.
    *   **Good Dataset and Real-World Application:** Utilizes anonymized inpatient records from a national acute hospital in Ireland.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Novelty:** The paper doesn't present groundbreaking new AI architectures or XAI methods. It primarily applies existing techniques to a new dataset. While the application is valuable, the methodological novelty is moderate.
    *   **Data Limitations:** The dataset suffers from class imbalance (low CPE-positive cases), potentially limiting the performance and generalizability of the models. The relatively coarse granularity of some data, such as bed-day level timestamps and ward-level contact networks, may also affect the accuracy of transmission modeling. Patient privacy restrictions prevents data linking across hospital systems, risk of mislabeling for some readmission patient.
    *   **Modest Performance Gains:** While Transformer-based models outperform baselines, the performance gains, particularly for precision-recall metrics, aren't dramatically high. This raises questions about the practical significance of these gains in a clinical setting.
    *   **Causality vs. Correlation:** The study primarily focuses on correlations. The interpretability provided by XAI does not guarantee causal insights, and findings need clinical validation to prevent misinterpretation.
    *   **Generalizability Concerns:** The analysis is limited to a single Irish hospital, which raises questions about the generalizability of the findings to other healthcare settings with different patient populations, IPC practices, and data collection systems.
    *   **Lack of Long-Term Impact Assessment:** It focuses on short-term outcomes (30-day readmission, etc.) and doesn't assess the long-term impact of interventions informed by the model.

*   **Significance:**

    The paper's significance lies in its demonstration of how XAI can be used to extract actionable insights from real-world EMR data to improve infection control. Even though it doesn't present new AI methodologies, its focus on explainability and practical application to a pressing healthcare challenge makes it relevant to the field of AI in healthcare and infection prevention. The identification of specific risk factors and potential transmission pathways can inform targeted interventions and improve patient safety.
*   **Rigorous Rationale:**
    * The comprehensive evaluation includes both quantitative results (using various metrics) and qualitative insights (through XAI methods and embedding visualizations).
    * The limitations of the study are acknowledged, including the limited novelty of the proposed framework and the challenges associated with data imbalance.
    * The clinical relevance of the findings is emphasized, with a focus on translating model predictions into actionable insights for infection prevention and control.
    * The importance of integrating contact network features is highlighted, but the need for further research in this area is also recognized.

**Score: 6**

**Rationale:**

The paper demonstrates a practical and valuable application of AI in healthcare, specifically in addressing the critical issue of CPE infections. However, its limited methodological novelty, data limitations, and modest performance gains prevent it from being considered a groundbreaking contribution. The emphasis on XAI and its potential for informing IPC strategies are commendable, but further research is needed to validate the findings and assess their long-term impact. It's a solid application of existing techniques with good methodology in a relevant domain but lacks significant breakthroughs.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 6/10

### Explicit vs. Implicit Biographies: Evaluating and Adapting LLM Information Extraction on Wikidata-Derived Texts
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14943v1)
- **Authors**: Alessandra Stramiglio, Andrea Schimmenti, Valentina Pasqual, Marieke van Erp, Francesco Sovrano, Fabio Vitali
- **Abstract**: Text Implicitness has always been challenging in Natural Language Processing (NLP), with traditional methods relying on explicit statements to identify entities and their relationships. From the sentence "Zuhdi attends church every Sunday", the relationship between Zuhdi and Christianity is evident for a human reader, but it presents a challenge when it must be inferred automatically. Large language models (LLMs) have proven effective in NLP downstream tasks such as text comprehension and information extraction (IE). This study examines how textual implicitness affects IE tasks in pre-trained LLMs: LLaMA 2.3, DeepSeekV1, and Phi1.5. We generate two synthetic datasets of 10k implicit and explicit verbalization of biographic information to measure the impact on LLM performance and analyze whether fine-tuning implicit data improves their ability to generalize in implicit reasoning tasks. This research presents an experiment on the internal reasoning processes of LLMs in IE, particularly in dealing with implicit and explicit contexts. The results demonstrate that fine-tuning LLM models with LoRA (low-rank adaptation) improves their performance in extracting information from implicit texts, contributing to better model interpretability and reliability.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores how well Large Language Models (LLMs) perform on information extraction (IE) tasks when the information is expressed implicitly versus explicitly.  The authors created synthetic datasets of biographical information, verbalized both explicitly and implicitly, and then fine-tuned several LLMs (LLaMA 3.2, DeepSeekV1, Phi1.5) using LoRA.  The experiments measured the impact of explicit vs. implicit data on LLM performance and investigated whether fine-tuning with implicit data improves the LLMs' ability to generalize to implicit reasoning. The results indicate that LLMs struggle with implicit information extraction compared to explicit information.  Fine-tuning with both explicit and implicit data improves performance on implicit extraction, suggesting the difficulty lies in insufficient exposure to implicit patterns during training.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper's novelty lies in its focused investigation of the explicit vs. implicit distinction in the context of LLMs and information extraction, especially with a specific emphasis on biographical data. Previous research has examined implicit knowledge in LLMs, but this work directly examines the effect through synthetic datasets, fine-tuning with LoRA, and a targeted focus on IE. While the core techniques (LLMs, fine-tuning, synthetic data) are well-established, their application to this specific question is novel. The creation and utilization of a synthetic biographical dataset designed to control for explicitness is a notable contribution.

*   **Significance:** The findings are significant because they highlight a persistent weakness of LLMs: difficulty in extracting information when it isn't stated directly.  Many real-world texts rely heavily on implicit communication, making this a crucial issue for the reliable deployment of LLMs in IE applications. Demonstrating that exposure to implicit data during fine-tuning improves performance is a valuable practical insight. The practical implications are clear: improving LLMs' ability to handle implicit knowledge is essential for building more robust and useful information extraction systems.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Research Questions:** The RQs are well-defined and address an important problem.
    *   **Methodology:** The methodology is rigorous, using controlled synthetic datasets, appropriate fine-tuning techniques (LoRA), and statistical comparisons. The use of synthetic data allows for careful manipulation of the explicitness variable.
    *   **Experimental Design:** The experiment is well-designed, allowing a clear comparison of LLM performance in various scenarios.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The authors provide code and data, enhancing the reproducibility of their findings.
    *   **Clear presentation of results:** The results are well presented using Tables and clearly discussed

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Synthetic Data Limitation:**  The synthetic nature of the data is both a strength (control) and a weakness.  While it allows for controlled experimentation, the generated text may not fully capture the complexities and nuances of implicit language in real-world human-written text. The generated Implicit datasets could include more variations of periphrasis, metonymy, deduction or other relevant rhetorical strategies.
    *   **Limited Scope:** The experiments focus solely on biographical information.  It is unclear if the findings would generalize to other types of text or domains.
    *   **LLM Model Selection:** While the choice of LLaMA, DeepSeek and Phi is motivated, focusing on a wider range of model sizes and architectures could broaden the scope of the findings.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in several ways:

    *   It emphasizes the need for datasets and training techniques that specifically address implicit knowledge.
    *   It provides a useful methodology for evaluating LLM performance on implicit reasoning.
    *   It motivates further exploration of different fine-tuning strategies and architectural modifications to improve LLM's handling of implicit information.

**Justification for Score:**

I'm assigning a score of **7** out of 10. While the paper employs well-established techniques (fine-tuning, synthetic data generation), it does so in a novel and well-designed manner to address a specific and practically important problem in information extraction. The rigorous methodology, clear results, and potential for future research warrant a high score. However, the limitations of synthetic data and a focused scope prevent it from reaching a higher tier. The paper offers practical insights and demonstrates a clear path towards improving LLMs ability to handle implicit language and information extraction.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Stochastic Bilevel Optimization with Heavy-Tailed Noise
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14952v1)
- **Authors**: Zhuanghua Liu, Luo Luo
- **Abstract**: This paper considers the smooth bilevel optimization in which the lower-level problem is strongly convex and the upper-level problem is possibly nonconvex. We focus on the stochastic setting that the algorithm can access the unbiased stochastic gradient evaluation with heavy-tailed noise, which is prevalent in many machine learning applications such as training large language models and reinforcement learning. We propose a nested-loop normalized stochastic bilevel approximation (N$^2$SBA) for finding an $\epsilon$-stationary point with the stochastic first-order oracle (SFO) complexity of $\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\big(\kappa^{\frac{7p-3}{p-1}} \sigma^{\frac{p}{p-1}} \epsilon^{-\frac{4 p - 2}{p-1}}\big)$, where $\kappa$ is the condition number, $p\in(1,2]$ is the order of central moment for the noise, and $\sigma$ is the noise level. Furthermore, we specialize our idea to solve the nonconvex-strongly-concave minimax optimization problem, achieving an $\epsilon$-stationary point with the SFO complexity of $\tilde{\mathcal O}\big(\kappa^{\frac{2p-1}{p-1}} \sigma^{\frac{p}{p-1}} \epsilon^{-\frac{3p-2}{p-1}}\big)$. All above upper bounds match the best-known results under the special case of the bounded variance setting, i.e., $p=2$.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses stochastic bilevel optimization in a setting where the lower-level problem is strongly convex and the upper-level problem can be nonconvex. A key challenge tackled is the presence of heavy-tailed noise in the stochastic gradient estimates, a common occurrence in many machine learning applications. The authors propose a "nested-loop normalized stochastic bilevel approximation" (N2SBA) algorithm. They establish that N2SBA achieves an ε-stationary point with a specified stochastic first-order oracle (SFO) complexity. Furthermore, they specialize the algorithm to solve nonconvex-strongly-concave minimax optimization problems, demonstrating that their approach matches the best-known results under bounded variance conditions and improves performance in heavy-tailed settings. In addition, the algorithm clips gradients in an inner loop to mitigate issues related to heavy-tailed gradients, and uses normalization to correct gradient bias in the outer loop. The method performs stochastic gradient descent on the original objective function unlike previous approaches.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the development of a bilevel optimization algorithm (N2SBA) that is provably convergent under heavy-tailed noise conditions. While existing methods address bilevel optimization, they often assume bounded gradient variance, a limitation in real-world scenarios. The theoretical analysis demonstrating the complexity bounds of N2SBA under heavy-tailed noise, and the matching of best-known results in the bounded variance case, is a significant contribution. Applying normalization with a two-timescale update is also a contribution over simple normalization, and gradient clipping addresses issues relating to exploding gradients.
* **Significance:** The presence of heavy-tailed noise is increasingly recognized in many machine learning problems, including LLM training and reinforcement learning. By providing an algorithm and corresponding analysis tailored to this setting, the paper addresses a practical and important gap in the literature. The derived complexity bounds are valuable for understanding the algorithm's performance and comparing it with existing approaches. Moreover, the focus on fully stochastic first-order methods enhances scalability, making the proposed algorithm applicable to large-scale bilevel optimization problems. The application of N2SBA to the nonconvex-strongly-concave minimax optimization problem further broadens its applicability.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Rigorous Theoretical Analysis:** The paper presents a clear and detailed theoretical analysis, including precise complexity bounds.
    * **Practical Relevance:** The focus on heavy-tailed noise makes the algorithm highly relevant to real-world machine learning applications.
    * **Scalability:**  The algorithm is fully stochastic and first-order, enhancing its potential for large-scale problems.
    * **Thorough Experimental Evaluation:** The experiments compare against other baselines, and validate the effectiveness of the algorithms.
* **Weaknesses:**
   * **Complexity of Implementation and Tuning:** Nested-loop algorithms can sometimes be challenging to implement and tune effectively. The performance of N2SBA might depend on careful selection of step sizes and clipping parameters.
   * **Specific Bilevel Structure:** The paper assumes a specific bilevel structure where the lower-level problem is strongly convex. While this is a common assumption, it limits the applicability of the results to more general bilevel problems.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field of bilevel optimization by providing a provably convergent algorithm for heavy-tailed noise settings. The theoretical analysis is rigorous, and the practical relevance is high. The key strength is that it addresses a significant limitation of existing methods that rely on bounded variance assumptions. The weaknesses primarily relate to potential practical challenges in implementation and the somewhat restrictive assumption of a strongly convex lower-level problem. Taking these factors into consideration, a score of 7 is appropriate. It addresses an important niche in optimization and can open the door for research in more complex scenarios.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Sentinel Agents for Secure and Trustworthy Agentic AI in Multi-Agent Systems
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14956v1)
- **Authors**: Diego Gosmar, Deborah A. Dahl
- **Abstract**: This paper proposes a novel architectural framework aimed at enhancing security and reliability in multi-agent systems (MAS). A central component of this framework is a network of Sentinel Agents, functioning as a distributed security layer that integrates techniques such as semantic analysis via large language models (LLMs), behavioral analytics, retrieval-augmented verification, and cross-agent anomaly detection. Such agents can potentially oversee inter-agent communications, identify potential threats, enforce privacy and access controls, and maintain comprehensive audit records. Complementary to the idea of Sentinel Agents is the use of a Coordinator Agent. The Coordinator Agent supervises policy implementation, and manages agent participation. In addition, the Coordinator also ingests alerts from Sentinel Agents. Based on these alerts, it can adapt policies, isolate or quarantine misbehaving agents, and contain threats to maintain the integrity of the MAS ecosystem. This dual-layered security approach, combining the continuous monitoring of Sentinel Agents with the governance functions of Coordinator Agents, supports dynamic and adaptive defense mechanisms against a range of threats, including prompt injection, collusive agent behavior, hallucinations generated by LLMs, privacy breaches, and coordinated multi-agent attacks. In addition to the architectural design, we present a simulation study where 162 synthetic attacks of different families (prompt injection, hallucination, and data exfiltration) were injected into a multi-agent conversational environment. The Sentinel Agents successfully detected the attack attempts, confirming the practical feasibility of the proposed monitoring approach. The framework also offers enhanced system observability, supports regulatory compliance, and enables policy evolution over time.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Sentinel Agents, a novel architectural framework designed to enhance security and reliability in multi-agent systems (MAS), particularly those using Agentic AI. Sentinel Agents function as a distributed security layer, employing techniques like semantic analysis via LLMs, behavioral analytics, retrieval-augmented verification, and cross-agent anomaly detection. They monitor inter-agent communication to identify threats, enforce policies, and maintain audit records. Complementary to Sentinel Agents is the Coordinator Agent, which supervises policy implementation, manages agent participation, and responds to alerts from Sentinel Agents by adapting policies or isolating misbehaving agents. The architecture aims to defend against prompt injection, collusive behavior, hallucinations, privacy breaches, and coordinated attacks. The paper includes a simulation study demonstrating the detection of various synthetic attacks in a multi-agent conversational environment.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core concept of Sentinel Agents as a dedicated security layer within MAS is reasonably novel. While individual techniques such as LLMs for semantic analysis and anomaly detection are not new, their integration within a distributed agent architecture specifically to address security concerns in conversational AI represents a worthwhile contribution. The focus on a layered defense, combining pre-validation, passive listening, and a hybrid approach, adds to the originality.

*   **Significance:** The paper tackles a critical and timely issue: the security and trustworthiness of increasingly complex MAS environments. As these systems become more prevalent, particularly in applications involving sensitive data or critical decision-making, the vulnerabilities highlighted (prompt injection, hallucinations, collusion) pose significant risks. The proposed Sentinel Agent framework offers a structured approach to mitigating these risks, potentially contributing to the responsible development and deployment of Agentic AI.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clearly defined architecture:** The paper provides a well-defined architectural framework with clear roles for Sentinel and Coordinator Agents, outlining various deployment patterns.
    *   **Comprehensive threat model:** The paper identifies and describes a range of relevant security and safety threats in MAS environments.
    *   **Practical focus:** The inclusion of a simulation study, while preliminary, demonstrates the practical feasibility of the monitoring approach and suggests concrete implementation details.
    *   **Integration with existing frameworks:** The comparison with existing AI security frameworks (NIST, OWASP, Google SAIF, ENISA FAICP) and agent communication protocols (MCP, A2A, ANP, SLOP) helps position the Sentinel Agent approach within the broader landscape.
    *   **Ethical considerations:** The paper acknowledges the ethical implications of deploying automated security mechanisms and discusses potential biases and the need for human oversight.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited evaluation:** The simulation study is relatively limited in scope, with a small number of hallucination probes and no measurement of false positives. It primarily focuses on proof-of-concept.
    *   **Lack of ablation studies:** The paper does not quantify the relative contributions of different components within the Sentinel Agent architecture (rule-based filters, behavioral analysis, LLMs), making it difficult to assess their individual effectiveness.
    *   **Implementation details:** While the paper presents a well-defined architectural framework, the actual implementation details, especially regarding the tuning and configuration of LLMs for semantic analysis, would greatly benefit from more concrete examples. The appendix provides some insight, but further elaboration is needed.
    *   **Scalability concerns:** Although the paper describes techniques for addressing scalability, the actual performance and resource requirements of deploying Sentinel Agents in large-scale, heterogeneous MAS environments remain unclear.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the design and development of more secure and trustworthy MAS environments. It provides a valuable framework for addressing emerging security challenges and encourages further research in this area. The emphasis on layered defenses, continuous monitoring, and adaptive policies aligns with best practices in cybersecurity. However, widespread adoption will depend on demonstrating the practical effectiveness and scalability of the Sentinel Agent approach in real-world scenarios.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:** The paper presents a reasonably novel and significant contribution to the field of AI security, specifically within the context of multi-agent systems. The concept of Sentinel Agents provides a structured approach to addressing emerging security threats in conversational AI. However, the limited evaluation, lack of ablation studies, and lack of discussion on the real-world constraints prevent it from being an exceptional contribution at this stage. Future work, as outlined by the authors, is needed to strengthen the empirical basis of the Sentinel Agent architecture and address potential limitations.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### FAWN: A MultiEncoder Fusion-Attention Wave Network for Integrated Sensing and Communication Indoor Scene Inference
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14968v1)
- **Authors**: Carlos Barroso-Fernández, Alejandro Calvillo-Fernandez, Antonio de la Oliva, Carlos J. Bernardos
- **Abstract**: The upcoming generations of wireless technologies promise an era where everything is interconnected and intelligent. As the need for intelligence grows, networks must learn to better understand the physical world. However, deploying dedicated hardware to perceive the environment is not always feasible, mainly due to costs and/or complexity. Integrated Sensing and Communication (ISAC) has made a step forward in addressing this challenge. Within ISAC, passive sensing emerges as a cost-effective solution that reuses wireless communications to sense the environment, without interfering with existing communications. Nevertheless, the majority of current solutions are limited to one technology (mostly Wi-Fi or 5G), constraining the maximum accuracy reachable. As different technologies work with different spectrums, we see a necessity in integrating more than one technology to augment the coverage area. Hence, we take the advantage of ISAC passive sensing, to present FAWN, a MultiEncoder Fusion-Attention Wave Network for ISAC indoor scene inference. FAWN is based on the original transformers architecture, to fuse information from Wi-Fi and 5G, making the network capable of understanding the physical world without interfering with the current communication. To test our solution, we have built a prototype and integrated it in a real scenario. Results show errors below 0.6 m around 84% of times.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "FAWN: A MultiEncoder Fusion-Attention Wave Network for Integrated Sensing and Communication Indoor Scene Inference":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces FAWN, a novel neural network architecture designed for indoor scene inference using Integrated Sensing and Communication (ISAC) principles. FAWN leverages passive sensing by fusing information from both Wi-Fi and 5G signals.  The architecture is based on a dual-encoder, single-decoder system inspired by the Transformer architecture.  Separate encoders process Wi-Fi beacon and 5G SSB CSI data, and a sensor fusion module, employing attention mechanisms, combines these representations. The decoder then uses this combined information to infer the presence and location of objects (robots and people) within an indoor environment.  The paper includes a real-world deployment and experimental evaluation, demonstrating FAWN's performance in terms of detection accuracy and localization precision.  Results indicate that FAWN achieves good accuracy and precision.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Novelty:** The paper's main strength lies in its novel approach to ISAC by fusing Wi-Fi and 5G data for passive sensing. Most existing research focuses on using either Wi-Fi or 5G independently. The combination of these technologies allows taking advantage of both of the technologies, improving the overall coverage and robustness.
    *   **Architecture:** The adoption of the Transformer architecture with specialized encoders and attention mechanisms is well-motivated. This design allows the network to effectively handle the heterogeneous nature of Wi-Fi and 5G signals and extract relevant features.
    *   **Real-world Deployment:** The evaluation is performed in a realistic laboratory environment, which enhances the practicality and relevance of the results. The real scenario uses commodity APs (Access Points), therefore, the solution is cheaper than other similar approaches.
    *   **Performance:** The experimental results demonstrate good performance in terms of detection accuracy and localization precision compared to other solutions.
    *   **Modularity:** The architecture is modular and well-suited for easy scaling.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Evaluation Scope:** While the deployment is real-world, the evaluation scenario is relatively constrained. The study focused on detecting and localizing only two object types (robots and people) and a 9x10m grid. A more comprehensive evaluation with varying environmental conditions (furniture arrangement, people density, robot types), and potentially more object types.
    *   **Baseline Comparison:** The baseline comparison is acceptable, using CNN-ABLSTM and variants with only Wi-Fi or 5G. However, a stronger comparison would include more state-of-the-art ISAC methods that explicitly address sensor fusion or multi-modal sensing. It focuses on one specific competitor and reduces the scope to only two competitors.
    *   **Limited information of the parameters:** The architecture of the neural network is explained with general terms, for example, the size of the convolutional kernels is not defined, or the dimensions of each layer. The explanation of how CSI signals are obtained is also not detailed enough.
    *   **Lack of ablation experiments:** The paper does not perform ablation experiments to understand the contribution of each component in the architecture.

*   **Significance:**
    *   FAWN's approach of fusing Wi-Fi and 5G for passive sensing has the potential to advance the field of ISAC by providing a more robust and accurate sensing solution.
    *   The modular architecture of FAWN makes it easily extensible to incorporate other sensing technologies in the future.
    *   The performance results are competitive, making FAWN a promising solution for real-world applications.

**Overall:**
The paper presents a novel and well-engineered solution to the problem of indoor scene inference using ISAC. The fusion of Wi-Fi and 5G data is a significant contribution, and the architecture is well-designed and achieves good performance. However, the evaluation scope is limited, and a more thorough comparison with state-of-the-art methods would strengthen the paper.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### What Matters in LLM-Based Feature Extractor for Recommender? A Systematic Analysis of Prompts, Models, and Adaptation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14979v1)
- **Authors**: Kainan Shi, Peilin Zhou, Ge Wang, Han Ding, Fei Wang
- **Abstract**: Using Large Language Models (LLMs) to generate semantic features has been demonstrated as a powerful paradigm for enhancing Sequential Recommender Systems (SRS). This typically involves three stages: processing item text, extracting features with LLMs, and adapting them for downstream models. However, existing methods vary widely in prompting, architecture, and adaptation strategies, making it difficult to fairly compare design choices and identify what truly drives performance. In this work, we propose RecXplore, a modular analytical framework that decomposes the LLM-as-feature-extractor pipeline into four modules: data processing, semantic feature extraction, feature adaptation, and sequential modeling. Instead of proposing new techniques, RecXplore revisits and organizes established methods, enabling systematic exploration of each module in isolation. Experiments on four public datasets show that simply combining the best designs from existing techniques without exhaustive search yields up to 18.7% relative improvement in NDCG@5 and 12.7% in HR@5 over strong baselines. These results underscore the utility of modular benchmarking for identifying effective design patterns and promoting standardized research in LLM-enhanced recommendation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "What Matters in LLM-Based Feature Extractor for Recommender? A Systematic Analysis of Prompts, Models, and Adaptation":

**Summary:**

The paper presents RecXplore, a modular framework designed for systematically analyzing the "LLM-as-feature-extractor" paradigm in sequential recommender systems (SRS).  RecXplore decomposes the pipeline into four core modules: Data Processing, Feature Extraction, Feature Adaptation, and Sequential Modeling. The authors conduct controlled experiments on four public datasets, exploring various design choices within each module. Their findings suggest that a simple combination of existing techniques (simple attribute concatenation, mean pooling, CPT+SFT fine-tuning, MoE adapter with PCA, and direct ID embedding replacement) performs surprisingly well, outperforming strong baselines without requiring complex architectures. The study emphasizes the utility of modular benchmarking for identifying effective design patterns in LLM-enhanced recommendation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Systematic Analysis:** The paper's core strength lies in its systematic and modular approach. By isolating and analyzing each component of the LLM-as-feature-extractor pipeline, the authors provide valuable insights that are often obscured in studies that propose end-to-end models. This framework allows for a more controlled comparison of different techniques.
*   **Reproducibility and Modularity:** The framework promotes reproducibility. The paper revisits existing techniques, not proposing entirely new ones, which enables standardization and future investigation. The modular design further enhances reproducibility.
*   **Empirical Validation:** The experiments are conducted on multiple datasets, lending robustness to the findings.  The comparison against strong baselines provides a clear benchmark for the performance of the proposed RecXplore.
*   **Practical Implications:** The finding that a combination of existing techniques can yield strong performance is significant.  It suggests that practitioners can improve their recommender systems without necessarily resorting to cutting-edge, complex architectures.
*   **Clear Research Questions:** The research questions are clearly defined, providing a strong structure for the experiments and analysis.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Novelty in Individual Components:** The paper largely revisits existing techniques. While the *combination* and *systematic analysis* are novel, the individual modules draw from well-established methods. The novelty lies primarily in *how* these are combined and analyzed rather than inventing entirely new techniques.
*   **Greedy Optimization:** The use of a greedy optimization strategy limits the potential to find the globally optimal configuration of designs. This is acknowledged by the authors. The best-performing design choices from each module, tested in isolation, are then combined in RecXplore. This approach could miss interactions between the module designs, and the best *overall* configuration may be different from the sum of the best designs of each isolated module.
*   **Focus on SASRec:**  The exclusive focus on the SASRec backbone limits the generalizability of the findings to other sequential recommendation models.  While SASRec is a common and powerful model, exploring the impact of different LLM-based feature extractors on other architectures would further strengthen the results.
*   **Lack of Deeper Explanation:** While the paper reports which components perform best, it offers limited *explanations* as to *why* certain components outperform others. This is especially true in the feature adaptation module. For example, why MoE outperforms other adaptation methods may benefit from further analysis.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper makes a significant contribution by introducing a systematic and reproducible framework for analyzing LLM-based feature extractors in recommender systems. The modular approach and comprehensive experiments provide valuable insights into the impact of different design choices. While the individual components are not entirely novel, the *combination* and *methodological rigor* represent a valuable advancement.

However, the paper's primary weakness lies in the limited novelty of the individual components and the greedy optimization approach. While practical, this approach could miss potentially superior global configurations. Also, further investigation into how the design choices of each module affects the other modules and SASRec could improve the performance and results of the overall model.

Given these considerations, I assign a score of **7**. The paper's strengths (systematic analysis, reproducibility, and practical implications) are considerable and advance the field. However, the limited novelty in the component techniques and greedy optimization preclude a higher score.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 7/10

### SPATIALGEN: Layout-guided 3D Indoor Scene Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14981v1)
- **Authors**: Chuan Fang, Heng Li, Yixun Liang, Jia Zheng, Yongsen Mao, Yuan Liu, Rui Tang, Zihan Zhou, Ping Tan
- **Abstract**: Creating high-fidelity 3D models of indoor environments is essential for applications in design, virtual reality, and robotics. However, manual 3D modeling remains time-consuming and labor-intensive. While recent advances in generative AI have enabled automated scene synthesis, existing methods often face challenges in balancing visual quality, diversity, semantic consistency, and user control. A major bottleneck is the lack of a large-scale, high-quality dataset tailored to this task. To address this gap, we introduce a comprehensive synthetic dataset, featuring 12,328 structured annotated scenes with 57,440 rooms, and 4.7M photorealistic 2D renderings. Leveraging this dataset, we present SpatialGen, a novel multi-view multi-modal diffusion model that generates realistic and semantically consistent 3D indoor scenes. Given a 3D layout and a reference image (derived from a text prompt), our model synthesizes appearance (color image), geometry (scene coordinate map), and semantic (semantic segmentation map) from arbitrary viewpoints, while preserving spatial consistency across modalities. SpatialGen consistently generates superior results to previous methods in our experiments. We are open-sourcing our data and models to empower the community and advance the field of indoor scene understanding and generation.
