- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SPATIALGEN, a novel framework for generating high-fidelity 3D indoor scenes conditioned on a 3D layout. The method leverages a multi-view multi-modal diffusion model trained on a newly created, large-scale synthetic dataset of indoor scenes.  SPATIALGEN synthesizes photorealistic RGB images, semantic segmentation maps, and scene coordinate maps from arbitrary viewpoints, ensuring spatial and semantic consistency. The method supports scene generation from text prompts, reference images, and even video, where the layout is estimated from the video using an existing layout estimator. The authors demonstrate superior results compared to existing methods, particularly in handling large viewpoint changes and maintaining semantic consistency.  They also open-source their dataset and models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Dataset Contribution:** The creation of a large-scale, high-quality synthetic dataset is a significant contribution. The lack of such datasets has been a bottleneck in the field, and this dataset addresses that gap directly. The detailed annotations, including layouts, scene coordinates, and panoramic renderings, make it a valuable resource for future research.
*   **Multi-Modal Diffusion:** The approach of jointly generating RGB images, semantic maps, and scene coordinate maps within a diffusion framework is well-motivated and allows for explicit 3D supervision and cross-view guidance.
*   **Layout-Guided Attention:** The design of the layout-guided attention mechanism, with alternating cross-view and cross-modal attention, is a clever way to enforce consistency and alignment across different modalities and viewpoints.
*   **Iterative Generation & Gaussian Splatting:** The iterative view generation and Gaussian splatting optimization effectively addresses the memory limitations and enables free-viewpoint rendering.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper includes thorough quantitative and qualitative evaluations against state-of-the-art methods on multiple datasets and camera trajectories. The ablation studies clearly demonstrate the benefits of the proposed components.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Synthetic Data Dependence:** The method relies on a synthetic dataset, which may limit its ability to generalize to real-world scenes with different lighting conditions, materials, and object appearances. While the authors aim for photorealism, a domain gap is always a concern.
*   **Computational Cost:** The multi-view diffusion model and iterative generation process are computationally intensive, as acknowledged by the authors. This might hinder its practicality for real-time applications.
*   **Incremental Novelty in Diffusion Architecture:** While the system as a whole is novel, the architectural modifications to the diffusion model (alternating attention) feel somewhat incremental. The core contribution is the data and the problem formulation, rather than a radical architectural breakthrough in diffusion modeling.
*   **Limited Layout Estimation:** The approach of using a separate layout estimator from video is a limitation. A more tightly integrated approach that jointly estimates layout and generates the scene would be more robust.

**Novelty and Significance:**

The paper's primary novelty lies in the combination of several aspects: (1) the large-scale, richly annotated dataset designed explicitly for layout-guided scene generation, (2) the multi-modal diffusion approach that leverages explicit 3D supervision, and (3) the system-level design, integrating layout estimation, iterative generation, and Gaussian splatting. While existing works have explored similar concepts, SPATIALGEN pushes the boundaries in terms of scale, completeness, and integration. The open-sourcing of the dataset will likely have a significant impact by enabling future research in this area.

The significance is in advancing the state-of-the-art in controllable 3D scene generation.  By addressing the data scarcity issue, the paper enables more realistic and consistent scene synthesis compared to previous methods. It provides a useful tool and benchmark for further exploration.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of **8**.  Here's the rationale:

*   The dataset contribution and the system-level integration are substantial and address a significant bottleneck in the field.  This justifies a strong positive assessment.
*   The results clearly demonstrate improvements over existing methods, particularly in spatial and semantic consistency.
*   The weaknesses related to synthetic data dependence and computational cost are important but do not outweigh the strengths.
*   The incremental architectural novelty in diffusion models prevents a higher rating (9 or 10), which would be reserved for breakthroughs that radically alter the core algorithmic landscape.
*   The dependency on a separate layout estimator is another limitation preventing a higher score, as a truly integrated approach would be more impressive.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### A Knowledge-driven Adaptive Collaboration of LLMs for Enhancing Medical Decision-making
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14998v1)
- **Authors**: Xiao Wu, Ting-Zhu Huang, Liang-Jian Deng, Yanyuan Qiao, Imran Razzak, Yutong Xie
- **Abstract**: Medical decision-making often involves integrating knowledge from multiple clinical specialties, typically achieved through multidisciplinary teams. Inspired by this collaborative process, recent work has leveraged large language models (LLMs) in multi-agent collaboration frameworks to emulate expert teamwork. While these approaches improve reasoning through agent interaction, they are limited by static, pre-assigned roles, which hinder adaptability and dynamic knowledge integration. To address these limitations, we propose KAMAC, a Knowledge-driven Adaptive Multi-Agent Collaboration framework that enables LLM agents to dynamically form and expand expert teams based on the evolving diagnostic context. KAMAC begins with one or more expert agents and then conducts a knowledge-driven discussion to identify and fill knowledge gaps by recruiting additional specialists as needed. This supports flexible, scalable collaboration in complex clinical scenarios, with decisions finalized through reviewing updated agent comments. Experiments on two real-world medical benchmarks demonstrate that KAMAC significantly outperforms both single-agent and advanced multi-agent methods, particularly in complex clinical scenarios (i.e., cancer prognosis) requiring dynamic, cross-specialty expertise. Our code is publicly available at: https://github.com/XiaoXiao-Woo/KAMAC.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "A Knowledge-driven Adaptive Collaboration of LLMs for Enhancing Medical Decision-making":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces KAMAC (Knowledge-driven Adaptive Multi-Agent Collaboration), a novel framework designed to improve medical decision-making by enabling Large Language Models (LLMs) to dynamically collaborate and expand their expert teams based on detected knowledge gaps.  Unlike previous multi-agent collaboration methods that rely on static, pre-assigned roles, KAMAC starts with one or more expert agents and engages in a knowledge-driven discussion.  If knowledge gaps are identified, additional specialists are recruited to fill them, leading to more flexible and scalable collaboration in complex clinical scenarios.  Decisions are finalized through a review of updated agent comments by a moderator. Experiments on medical benchmarks (MedQA and Progn-VQA) demonstrate that KAMAC outperforms single-agent and existing multi-agent approaches, particularly in complex scenarios requiring diverse expertise.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the *adaptive* aspect of the multi-agent collaboration. While multi-agent systems and LLMs for medical tasks are not new, KAMAC's ability to dynamically adjust the team composition based on identified knowledge gaps is a significant improvement. This is more closely aligned with how real-world medical teams function. The knowledge-driven recruitment is also a strength, as it provides a systematic approach to bringing in relevant expertise as needed. However, the novelty is somewhat incremental, building on existing multi-agent and medical LLM work.

*   **Significance:**  The potential impact of the work is considerable. Accurate medical decision-making is paramount, and enhancing LLMs to perform more effectively in this domain is highly valuable. KAMAC's ability to handle complex scenarios with diverse expertise needs is particularly relevant. The gains demonstrated on real-world benchmarks provide evidence of its potential. The paper addresses a key limitation of existing multi-agent systems: their inflexibility and reliance on static roles. A more adaptive approach like KAMAC could make these systems more practical for real-world deployment.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Adaptive Collaboration:** The core concept of dynamically adapting the expert team is well-motivated and implemented.
    *   **Knowledge-Driven Recruitment:**  Using knowledge gaps to drive expert recruitment provides a systematic and intelligent approach.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The benchmarks used are relevant, and the results demonstrate clear improvements over existing methods.
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:**  The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing approaches and articulates how KAMAC addresses them.
    *  **Well-defined Prompts:** The Appendices provide detail about the prompts used in the paper, enabling future researchers to replicate their setup and build upon their work.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Model Dependency:** The reliance on GPT-4.1-mini (or other specific LLMs) could be a limitation.  While the authors mention generalizability to DeepSeek-R1, exploring performance with a wider variety of LLMs (especially open-source models) would strengthen the findings.  The choice of GPT-4.1-mini may have biased results.
    *   **Complexity of prompts**: The approach requires careful crafting of prompts. There is likely sensitivity in the performance to the exact wording and content of prompts that were used.
    *   **Potential for Overfitting:**  The adaptive recruitment process, while beneficial, introduces the risk of overfitting to the training data or specific benchmark scenarios.  More rigorous analysis of generalization ability beyond the tested datasets would be helpful.
    *   **Limited Real-World Validation:** While the benchmarks are relevant, demonstrating KAMAC's effectiveness in a real-world clinical setting would significantly increase its impact.
    *  **Cost**: While the authors evaluate cost, there is a non-trivial expense that may become unsustainable with more complex, multi-agent interactions.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the design of future medical decision-support systems based on LLMs. Its adaptive collaboration approach could be adopted in other domains where complex reasoning and diverse expertise are required.  The focus on knowledge gaps as a driver for collaboration is also a valuable contribution that could inspire new research directions.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the points above, the paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of medical LLMs and multi-agent collaboration. While the novelty is somewhat incremental, the adaptive nature of KAMAC and its strong experimental results justify a favorable assessment. The weaknesses, such as the dependency on specific LLMs and the lack of real-world validation, temper the enthusiasm. Overall, the paper is a solid piece of work with the potential to influence future research and development.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Sea-ing Through Scattered Rays: Revisiting the Image Formation Model for Realistic Underwater Image Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15011v1)
- **Authors**: Vasiliki Ismiroglou, Malte Pedersen, Stefan H. Bengtson, Andreas Aakerberg, Thomas B. Moeslund
- **Abstract**: In recent years, the underwater image formation model has found extensive use in the generation of synthetic underwater data. Although many approaches focus on scenes primarily affected by discoloration, they often overlook the model's ability to capture the complex, distance-dependent visibility loss present in highly turbid environments. In this work, we propose an improved synthetic data generation pipeline that includes the commonly omitted forward scattering term, while also considering a nonuniform medium. Additionally, we collected the BUCKET dataset under controlled turbidity conditions to acquire real turbid footage with the corresponding reference images. Our results demonstrate qualitative improvements over the reference model, particularly under increasing turbidity, with a selection rate of 82. 5\% by survey participants. Data and code can be accessed on the project page: vap.aau.dk/sea-ing-through-scattered-rays.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of realistically generating synthetic underwater images, particularly in turbid environments. It argues that existing methods often overlook the importance of forward scattering and non-uniformity of the medium, leading to visually unrealistic results. The authors propose an improved synthetic data generation pipeline that includes the forward scattering term, considers non-uniformity through Gaussian Random Fields, and introduces adjustments to attenuation coefficients. To validate their approach, they collect the BUCKET dataset, a controlled turbidity dataset with corresponding reference images. Qualitative results and a user study demonstrate that their method produces more realistic images compared to a standard Underwater Image Formation Model (IFM), especially in high-turbidity conditions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the reintroduction of the forward scattering term and the incorporation of medium non-uniformity into the synthetic data generation pipeline. While the IFM itself is well-established, the paper makes a practical argument for including previously omitted components and explores a new approach of medium inhomogeneity, that has a direct impact to final images. The experimental validation through the BUCKET dataset adds value.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the improved realism of generated synthetic data. More realistic synthetic data can benefit various underwater computer vision tasks, such as image enhancement, object detection, and scene reconstruction, by providing better training data for learning-based methods. The BUCKET dataset is a contribution to the field, providing a benchmark for evaluating synthetic data generation techniques.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear problem definition:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing synthetic data generation methods for turbid underwater environments.
    *   **Well-reasoned approach:** The authors provide a logical justification for reintroducing the forward scattering term and incorporating medium non-uniformity.
    *   **Experimental validation:** The BUCKET dataset and the user study provide evidence to support the effectiveness of their proposed method.
    *   **Comprehensive analysis:** The paper provides a detailed review of related work and discusses the implications of their findings.
    *   **Dataset and code release:** The promised availability of the dataset and code enhances reproducibility and enables further research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited quantitative evaluation:** While the user study provides subjective feedback, the paper lacks more rigorous quantitative evaluation using established metrics for image quality or task performance (e.g., training a restoration network on generated data and testing on real data).
    *   **Simplified reference model:** The reference model is a simplified version of what's defined in Sec. 3, which makes it an inaccurate benchmark for measuring the improvements.
    *   **Depth estimation reliance:** The results rely heavily on the accuracy of the depth estimation model (Depth-Anything), and the impact of errors in depth estimation on the overall realism of the synthetic data is not thoroughly explored.
    *   **Specificity of BUCKET dataset:** The BUCKET dataset, while valuable, is limited by the controlled lab environment. Its generalizability to more complex real-world underwater scenes is unclear.
    *   **Parameter tuning:** The reliance on visual approximation for turbidity coefficients in the BUCKET dataset, introduces a subjective element and might affect the reliability of the results.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence the development of more realistic underwater image simulation techniques, which can lead to improved performance of underwater computer vision systems. The BUCKET dataset can serve as a valuable resource for the community.

*   **Justification for Score:** The paper makes a tangible contribution to the field by addressing a specific limitation in synthetic underwater image generation. The reintroduction of forward scattering and inclusion of medium non-uniformity, coupled with experimental validation, demonstrate the effectiveness of their approach. However, the limitations in quantitative evaluation, reliance on depth estimation, and the specificity of the BUCKET dataset prevent it from achieving a higher score. There's room for improvement in terms of rigor and generalizability.

**Score: 6.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Mind the Gap: A Closer Look at Tokenization for Multiple-Choice Question Answering with LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15020v1)
- **Authors**: Mario Sanz-Guerrero, Minh Duc Bui, Katharina von der Wense
- **Abstract**: When evaluating large language models (LLMs) with multiple-choice question answering (MCQA), it is common to end the prompt with the string "Answer:" to facilitate automated answer extraction via next-token probabilities. However, there is no consensus on how to tokenize the space following the colon, often overlooked as a trivial choice. In this paper, we uncover accuracy differences of up to 11% due to this (seemingly irrelevant) tokenization variation as well as reshuffled model rankings, raising concerns about the reliability of LLM comparisons in prior work. Surprisingly, we are able to recommend one specific strategy -- tokenizing the space together with the answer letter -- as we observe consistent and statistically significant performance improvements. Additionally, it improves model calibration, enhancing the reliability of the model's confidence estimates. Our findings underscore the importance of careful evaluation design and highlight the need for standardized, transparent evaluation protocols to ensure reliable and comparable results.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the impact of a seemingly trivial detail in evaluating Large Language Models (LLMs) on multiple-choice question answering (MCQA) tasks: the tokenization of the space character immediately following the "Answer:" prompt and preceding the option letter. The authors demonstrate that this tokenization choice, whether to tokenize the space separately or together with the option letter, can lead to accuracy differences of up to 11%, reshuffled model rankings on leaderboards, and variations in model calibration. They recommend tokenizing the space together with the answer letter, observing consistent accuracy and calibration improvements across various LLMs and datasets. The study highlights the importance of careful evaluation design and the need for standardized evaluation protocols to ensure reliable LLM comparisons.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper addresses a subtle yet important aspect of LLM evaluation that has been largely overlooked in the existing literature. While previous works have focused on prompt engineering and its effects on LLM performance, the impact of *tokenization* of the space character in a specific context (MCQA evaluation) is a novel contribution. This is particularly relevant given the increasing reliance on automated evaluation metrics and leaderboards.

*   **Significance:** The findings have significant implications for the field of LLM evaluation. The observed performance variations, altered model rankings, and changes in calibration highlight the instability of current evaluation methodologies. This raises concerns about the validity of previous LLM comparisons and underscores the need for more standardized and transparent evaluation protocols. The recommended tokenization strategy offers a practical solution to mitigate these issues. The fact that such a small change can cause such a large difference shows the maturity level of these models.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Empirical Evidence:** The paper presents comprehensive empirical evidence across a wide range of LLMs, datasets, and prompt variations.
    *   **Statistical Significance:** The authors rigorously analyze their results using statistical tests to demonstrate the significance of the observed differences.
    *   **Practical Recommendation:** The paper offers a clear and actionable recommendation for practitioners in the field.
    *   **Focus on Calibration:** The analysis extends beyond accuracy to include model calibration, providing a more holistic view of the impact of tokenization.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope:** The study primarily focuses on open-weight models due to the need for access to next-token logits. The findings may not be directly generalizable to proprietary, API-based models without further investigation.
    *   **Model Size:** While a range of models are tested, the majority are considered small- to medium-sized, peaking with models at 72B parameters. While general trends are likely consistent, it would be valuable to confirm these patterns with larger models as they become more accessible.
    *   **Lack of Theoretical Explanation:** While the paper presents a convincing empirical demonstration, it doesn't offer a definitive theoretical explanation for *why* this specific tokenization choice leads to improved performance. The authors suggest similarity between tokens as a potential factor, but a more in-depth exploration would be beneficial.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence the development of more standardized and robust LLM evaluation frameworks. It will encourage researchers and practitioners to pay closer attention to low-level implementation details and to conduct sensitivity analyses to ensure the reliability of their results. The clear findings and easy-to-implement solution make this paper highly useful.

**Score:** 7

**Justification:**

The paper makes a novel and practically significant contribution to the field of LLM evaluation by uncovering the impact of a previously overlooked tokenization detail. The rigorous empirical analysis and actionable recommendation are valuable strengths. However, the limitations regarding model scope, lack of theoretical explanation, and reliance on open-source models temper the paper's overall impact. The paper is important, but could be improved with a focus on these items. Therefore, a score of 7 seems reasonable.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### CLEAR: A Comprehensive Linguistic Evaluation of Argument Rewriting by Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15027v1)
- **Authors**: Thomas Huber, Christina Niklaus
- **Abstract**: While LLMs have been extensively studied on general text generation tasks, there is less research on text rewriting, a task related to general text generation, and particularly on the behavior of models on this task. In this paper we analyze what changes LLMs make in a text rewriting setting. We focus specifically on argumentative texts and their improvement, a task named Argument Improvement (ArgImp). We present CLEAR: an evaluation pipeline consisting of 57 metrics mapped to four linguistic levels: lexical, syntactic, semantic and pragmatic. This pipeline is used to examine the qualities of LLM-rewritten arguments on a broad set of argumentation corpora and compare the behavior of different LLMs on this task and analyze the behavior of different LLMs on this task in terms of linguistic levels. By taking all four linguistic levels into consideration, we find that the models perform ArgImp by shortening the texts while simultaneously increasing average word length and merging sentences. Overall we note an increase in the persuasion and coherence dimensions.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "CLEAR: A Comprehensive Linguistic Evaluation of Argument Rewriting by Large Language Models" presents a comprehensive evaluation of how Large Language Models (LLMs) perform on the task of Argument Improvement (ArgImp), which involves rewriting argumentative texts to enhance their overall quality. The authors introduce CLEAR, an evaluation pipeline comprising 57 metrics mapped to four linguistic levels (lexical, syntactic, semantic, and pragmatic). They use this pipeline to analyze LLM-rewritten arguments from various argumentation corpora, comparing the behavior of different LLMs and analyzing their performance across different linguistic levels. The study finds that LLMs typically shorten texts while increasing average word length and merging sentences. They also observe an increase in persuasion and coherence.  The paper also investigates potential biases related to length and positivity.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Strengths:**

    * **Comprehensive Evaluation Pipeline:** The primary strength is the CLEAR pipeline.  The meticulous mapping of metrics to linguistic levels and its application to a diverse range of LLMs and datasets represents a thorough approach to evaluating text rewriting. The incorporation of lexical, syntactic, semantic, and pragmatic metrics provides a multi-faceted view of LLM behavior.
    * **Focus on Argumentative Texts:**  The specific focus on ArgImp is a valuable contribution.  While LLMs have been extensively studied in general text generation, their behavior on more specialized tasks like argument refinement is less understood. This paper addresses that gap.
    * **Bias Analysis:** The investigation of length and positivity biases adds another layer of depth. Identifying potential biases in LLM outputs is critical for ensuring fair and reliable applications.
    * **Dataset Diversity:** Utilizing several argumentative corpora (Microtexts, Argument Annotated Essays, ArgRewrite) strengthens the generalizability of the findings.
    * **Open-Source Pipeline:** Making the CLEAR pipeline open-source is a significant contribution. It provides the community with a valuable tool for future research in this area.

* **Weaknesses:**

    * **Reliance on Automatic Metrics:** While the use of automatic metrics is necessary for large-scale evaluation, these metrics often fail to capture subtle but important aspects of argument quality. The reliance on automatic measures might skew the findings.  A larger or more in-depth qualitative analysis could strengthen the findings. Although they use 10 texts, this amount may not fully address the bias that exists due to the high reliance of automatic metrics for their evaluation.
    * **Limited Manual Analysis:** The manual analysis, while helpful, only covers a small subset of the generated texts. A more extensive qualitative analysis would provide deeper insights into the nature of the improvements and potential shortcomings of the LLMs.
    * **Subjectivity of Improvement:** "Improvement" in argument quality is inherently subjective and context-dependent. While the authors acknowledge the distinction between intrinsic and extrinsic quality, the evaluation largely focuses on measurable linguistic aspects, potentially overlooking aspects that readers find more persuasive or effective. A user study to assess perceived quality would add value.
    * **Limited Model Selection:** Model selection, while diverse, may not cover the most advanced or recent models.

* **Novelty and Significance:**

    * **Incremental Novelty:** The novelty of the paper lies primarily in its systematic application of a comprehensive evaluation pipeline to the specific task of ArgImp. While many of the individual metrics used are established, their combined use and mapping to linguistic levels within this context is a novel contribution.
    * **Limited Theoretical Insight:** The paper focuses primarily on empirical observation and analysis, without offering significant new theoretical insights into the mechanisms by which LLMs perform argument rewriting or the factors that influence their effectiveness.
    * **Practical Value:** The study has practical value in informing the development and deployment of LLM-based tools for argument writing support. The findings can help guide prompt engineering, model selection, and the design of evaluation metrics for ArgImp systems.  The release of the CLEAR pipeline has a significant practical impact.

* **Potential Influence:**

    * **Benchmarking and Comparison:** CLEAR could become a standard benchmark for evaluating LLMs on ArgImp.
    * **Future Research:** The findings can inform future research on LLM-based argument generation, bias mitigation, and quality assessment.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of **7** to this paper.  The comprehensive nature of the CLEAR pipeline, the focus on ArgImp, the bias analysis, and the open-sourcing of the pipeline represent valuable contributions.  However, the reliance on automatic metrics, the limited manual analysis, and lack of profound theoretical insight somewhat limit its overall significance. The paper provides a strong *empirical* contribution and a useful *tool*, but the novelty and *theoretical* contribution are more incremental.
Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### AutoEdit: Automatic Hyperparameter Tuning for Image Editing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15031v1)
- **Authors**: Chau Pham, Quan Dao, Mahesh Bhosale, Yunjie Tian, Dimitris Metaxas, David Doermann
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in diffusion models have revolutionized text-guided image editing, yet existing editing methods face critical challenges in hyperparameter identification. To get the reasonable editing performance, these methods often require the user to brute-force tune multiple interdependent hyperparameters, such as inversion timesteps and attention modification, \textit{etc.} This process incurs high computational costs due to the huge hyperparameter search space. We consider searching optimal editing's hyperparameters as a sequential decision-making task within the diffusion denoising process. Specifically, we propose a reinforcement learning framework, which establishes a Markov Decision Process that dynamically adjusts hyperparameters across denoising steps, integrating editing objectives into a reward function. The method achieves time efficiency through proximal policy optimization while maintaining optimal hyperparameter configurations. Experiments demonstrate significant reduction in search time and computational overhead compared to existing brute-force approaches, advancing the practical deployment of a diffusion-based image editing framework in the real world.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "AutoEdit: Automatic Hyperparameter Tuning for Image Editing" addresses the challenge of manually tuning hyperparameters in text-guided image editing using diffusion models. The authors propose a reinforcement learning (RL) framework where hyperparameter selection is framed as a sequential decision-making task within the diffusion denoising process.  A Markov Decision Process (MDP) is established to dynamically adjust hyperparameters at each denoising step, with a reward function incorporating prompt alignment and background preservation. Proximal Policy Optimization (PPO) is used for efficient optimization, reducing the search complexity from exponential to linear. Experiments demonstrate a reduction in search time and computational overhead compared to brute-force methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of framing hyperparameter tuning for diffusion-based image editing as a Reinforcement Learning problem and integrating this into the denoising process is reasonably novel. It moves beyond the common brute-force approaches. The specific implementation, using PPO and a carefully designed reward function, also adds some originality.

*   **Significance:**  The significance lies in the potential to automate and streamline the image editing process, making it more accessible to non-expert users. The reported reduction in search time compared to brute-force methods is a practical advantage. Automating hyperparameter tuning is a common pain point in many ML applications. This specific approach addresses image editing using diffusion models.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear problem formulation: The paper effectively highlights the limitations of manual hyperparameter tuning.
    *   Well-defined RL framework:  The MDP formulation and reward function design are logical and well-explained.
    *   Empirical validation:  The experiments show a reduction in search time and computational cost compared to brute-force baselines across various editing methods.
    *   Address an important usability issue: Manual hyperparameter tuning is a significant barrier for widespread adoption of diffusion-based editing.
    * Ablation study provides important insights

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Limited novelty in RL techniques: The RL methods used (PPO) are standard. The main contribution is the *application* of RL to this specific problem, not the development of new RL algorithms.
    *   Reliance on a well-defined reward function: The success of the approach hinges on the quality of the reward function. Although the authors use a combination of CLIP score and MSE, this is still a somewhat heuristic approach. The ablation study shows the importance of balancing prompt alignmen and background preservation.
    *   Scope limited to specific hyperparameters: While the paper mentions multiple hyperparameters, the experiments seem to focus more on inversion timesteps and attention ratios, which may not be the most challenging hyperparameters to tune. It is not clear if AutoEdit can deal with complicated interactions between hyperparameters.
    *   The gain is limited: the computational gains while significant are not ground breaking. There is still a significant computational cost to training. The cost of finding the optimal number of trials is also not considered.
    * Lack of a discussion of the failure case shown in Figure 8

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in automated machine learning for image editing and other generative model tasks.  It presents a viable alternative to grid search and manual tuning. Other RL methods may also be suitable or have superior performance.

*   **Rigorous Rationale:** The paper is well-written, the method is clearly defined, and the experiments provide some evidence for its effectiveness. However, the RL method used is relatively standard, and the reliance on a well-designed reward function is a limitation.  While automating hyperparameter tuning is significant, the empirical validation shows significant, but not exceptional, improvements. In addition, some important limitations are not well discussed. Therefore, a high score is not warranted.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Communication Efficient Split Learning of ViTs with Attention-based Double Compression
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15058v1)
- **Authors**: Federico Alvetreti, Jary Pomponi, Paolo Di Lorenzo, Simone Scardapane
- **Abstract**: This paper proposes a novel communication-efficient Split Learning (SL) framework, named Attention-based Double Compression (ADC), which reduces the communication overhead required for transmitting intermediate Vision Transformers activations during the SL training process. ADC incorporates two parallel compression strategies. The first one merges samples' activations that are similar, based on the average attention score calculated in the last client layer; this strategy is class-agnostic, meaning that it can also merge samples having different classes, without losing generalization ability nor decreasing final results. The second strategy follows the first and discards the least meaningful tokens, further reducing the communication cost. Combining these strategies not only allows for sending less during the forward pass, but also the gradients are naturally compressed, allowing the whole model to be trained without additional tuning or approximations of the gradients. Simulation results demonstrate that Attention-based Double Compression outperforms state-of-the-art SL frameworks by significantly reducing communication overheads while maintaining high accuracy.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Attention-based Double Compression (ADC), a new communication-efficient Split Learning (SL) framework designed to reduce the communication overhead when training Vision Transformers (ViTs).  ADC employs a two-pronged approach: first, it merges activations from similar samples based on the average attention score of the class token; and second, it discards the least important tokens within the merged activations.  The paper demonstrates through experiments on CIFAR100 and Food101 datasets that ADC outperforms existing SL frameworks in reducing communication overhead while maintaining high accuracy, particularly in scenarios with stringent communication limitations.  The approach is class-agnostic and avoids the need for gradient approximation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*Novelty:*

The novelty of this paper lies in its combined approach to compression within the SL setting for ViTs.  The two-stage compression strategy, leveraging attention scores for both sample merging and token selection, is a key differentiator. While techniques like token selection (Top-K, RandTopK) and batch compression (C3-SL) exist, the combined use and especially the attention-based sample merging offer a unique perspective. The class-agnostic nature of merging samples with potentially different labels based on attention similarity, is novel. This could be a potentially useful direction in cases where the class labels are uncertain or noisy.

*Significance:*

The significance of this work stems from its ability to address a crucial challenge in SL: reducing communication overhead.  The paper demonstrates improved performance compared to established methods, which is a strong indicator of its potential impact. By significantly reducing the amount of data transmitted during training, ADC makes SL more practical for resource-constrained edge devices.  The stable convergence behavior, observed during training, may be an interesting secondary contribution. However, it’s still worth investigating if this behavior extends to more diverse datasets, architectures, and training scenarios.

*Strengths:*

*   The paper presents a clear and well-structured methodology.
*   The experimental setup is comprehensive, including comparisons against relevant baselines.
*   The results convincingly demonstrate the superiority of ADC, especially under stringent communication constraints.
*   The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of different components of the proposed method (impact of batch size, alternative methods of batch merging).
*   The qualitative analysis with attention rollouts aids in understanding the inner workings of the method.

*Weaknesses:*

*   The evaluation is limited to CIFAR100 and Food101 datasets.  While these are common benchmarks, they are relatively small. Evaluating on larger and more complex datasets like ImageNet would strengthen the paper's claims.
*   The paper provides limited discussion or analysis on how ADC scales with increasing model size and data complexity. It mentions adapting the framework to federated learning in the future, but it doesn’t analyze the overhead or other parameters in this setting.
*   The work could benefit from a more in-depth discussion of the limitations. For instance, is the attention score always a reliable indicator of token importance across diverse scenarios? What are the failure modes? How does the performance vary across classes?
*   The implementation details should be more clear and complete. E.g., is the cluster selection made online at the end of each forward pass?  More information on parameter choices or the K-means implementations could improve the work's reproducibility.

*Potential Influence:*

ADC's potential lies in making SL more viable for real-world applications on edge devices. It provides a tangible solution to the communication bottleneck, making complex models such as ViTs trainable in these settings. The attention-based compression strategy could also inspire new approaches to communication-efficient distributed learning.

*Overall Assessment:*

The paper offers a valuable contribution to communication-efficient split learning for ViTs. The proposed ADC framework is innovative, well-evaluated, and shows promising results. The approach is well motivated and presents a practical solution for reducing communication overhead, an issue that can hinder the deployment of computationally intensive models on resource-constrained devices. Given its clear strengths and well-defined limitations, the paper represents a solid contribution.

### QuizRank: Picking Images by Quizzing VLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15059v1)
- **Authors**: Tenghao Ji, Eytan Adar
- **Abstract**: Images play a vital role in improving the readability and comprehension of Wikipedia articles by serving as `illustrative aids.' However, not all images are equally effective and not all Wikipedia editors are trained in their selection. We propose QuizRank, a novel method of image selection that leverages large language models (LLMs) and vision language models (VLMs) to rank images as learning interventions. Our approach transforms textual descriptions of the article's subject into multiple-choice questions about important visual characteristics of the concept. We utilize these questions to quiz the VLM: the better an image can help answer questions, the higher it is ranked. To further improve discrimination between visually similar items, we introduce a Contrastive QuizRank that leverages differences in the features of target (e.g., a Western Bluebird) and distractor concepts (e.g., Mountain Bluebird) to generate questions. We demonstrate the potential of VLMs as effective visual evaluators by showing a high congruence with human quiz-takers and an effective discriminative ranking of images.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "QuizRank: Picking Images by Quizzing VLMs":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces QuizRank, a novel method for selecting effective images for Wikipedia articles. It leverages Vision Language Models (VLMs) to rank images based on their ability to answer multiple-choice questions derived from the article's textual content. The approach transforms textual descriptions of the article's subject into multiple-choice questions about key visual characteristics of the concept. The VLM is "quizzed" using these questions and the candidate images; the images that best enable the VLM to answer the questions correctly are ranked higher. To improve discrimination between visually similar images, the authors propose a Contrastive QuizRank, which uses questions generated based on the differences between the target concept and related "distractor" concepts. The authors demonstrate the potential of VLMs as effective visual evaluators by showing high congruence between VLM and human quiz-takers and an effective discriminative ranking of images.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its innovative application of VLMs for image selection within the specific context of Wikipedia articles. Prior work has often focused on simple text-image similarity metrics or prompting VLMs to evaluate image fit directly. Transforming the task into a "quiz" for VLMs is a clever approach that better captures the image's ability to serve as an educational aid. The contrastive question generation aspect, focusing on visual differences with similar concepts, adds another layer of sophistication, improving the system's ability to select truly informative images. The use of well-established MCQ design principles in combination with state-of-the-art LLMs and VLMs adds to the robustness of the approach.

**Significance:** The paper addresses a practical problem of significant scale: selecting appropriate images from a vast pool of candidates to improve the quality and comprehension of Wikipedia articles. It also offers a potentially scalable and automated solution. The use of VLM as a proxy for human evaluation is insightful. The results suggesting congruence between VLM and human performance are promising and support the claim that VLM can be a viable visual evaluator. Showing that the system can distinguish between relevant and distractor images is essential. In a broader sense, it contributes to the growing body of research exploring the use of LLMs and VLMs in automated content creation and curation.

**Strengths:**

*   **Well-defined problem:**  Clearly articulates the challenges of image selection in Wikipedia and the need for automated solutions.
*   **Novel approach:** QuizRank provides an original take on using VLMs for image evaluation, moving beyond simple similarity or prompting.
*   **Contrastive learning:** Incorporating contrastive question generation improves the system's discriminative capabilities.
*   **Solid experimental validation:** The paper presents convincing evidence of the VLM's ability to mirror human perception and effectively rank images.
*   **Practical applicability:** The system's output can be used directly by Wikipedia editors to improve article quality.
*   **Analysis of VLM Memorization:** Addresses a relevant potential issue through controlled experiments regarding VLM memorization of training data.
*   **In-Class Representation Analysis:** Comparison of images ranked by QuizRank vs those most popularly used in Wikipedia provides justification for the method's value.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Scope:** While the experimental validation is solid, it is still limited in scope. The study could benefit from a larger-scale deployment within Wikipedia, including user studies that measure the impact on user comprehension and satisfaction.
*   **Generalizability to Other Domains:** The paper focuses solely on Wikipedia images. While the method is likely applicable in other domains, the specific implementation (question generation, distractor selection) may need adjustment.
*   **Dependency on LLM/VLM Performance:** The system's performance is inherently tied to the capabilities of the underlying LLM and VLM. As these models evolve, the system may require adaptation. It may benefit from benchmarking against other, more computationally-efficient, similarity or indexing methods.
*   **Ethical Considerations:** Although mentioned, the discussion on bias in LLMs and VLMs is limited. A more thorough investigation into potential biases in image selection and presentation is needed. What biases may be present in LLMs (e.g. gender/race representation). How may cultural visual references affect question generation/answering.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper has the potential to significantly influence how images are selected for Wikipedia articles and similar online resources. The automated approach could improve the quality and consistency of image selection, leading to more engaging and informative content. The use of contrastive question generation provides a template for improving the ability of VLMs to distinguish between visually similar objects. The system shows strong and reliable results in comparison to human annotation.

**Score:** 7.5

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and well-executed approach to a significant problem. The experimental results are encouraging and suggest the potential for broad impact. While limitations exist, they primarily pertain to scope and generalizability, rather than fundamental flaws in the methodology. The QuizRank approach provides an innovative and highly capable AI framework for enhancing Wikipedia's visual content. The strong overlap in image selection with human test-takers also lends credence to the method's capabilities in enhancing visual resources and content.

- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 7/10

### Learning in Context: Personalizing Educational Content with Large Language Models to Enhance Student Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15068v1)
- **Authors**: Joy Jia Yin Lim, Daniel Zhang-Li, Jifan Yu, Xin Cong, Ye He, Zhiyuan Liu, Huiqin Liu, Lei Hou, Juanzi Li, Bin Xu
- **Abstract**: Standardized, one-size-fits-all educational content often fails to connect with students' individual backgrounds and interests, leading to disengagement and a perceived lack of relevance. To address this challenge, we introduce PAGE, a novel framework that leverages large language models (LLMs) to automatically personalize educational materials by adapting them to each student's unique context, such as their major and personal interests. To validate our approach, we deployed PAGE in a semester-long intelligent tutoring system and conducted a user study to evaluate its impact in an authentic educational setting. Our findings show that students who received personalized content demonstrated significantly improved learning outcomes and reported higher levels of engagement, perceived relevance, and trust compared to those who used standardized materials. This work demonstrates the practical value of LLM-powered personalization and offers key design implications for creating more effective, engaging, and trustworthy educational experiences.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper, along with a score:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces PAGE, a framework designed to personalize educational content using large language models (LLMs).  PAGE aims to address the limitations of standardized educational materials by adapting them to students' individual backgrounds, interests, and contexts. The framework incorporates three key components: (1) Cognitive-Affective Student Profiling (2) Open-Ended Knowledge Retrieval and (3) Pedagogically-Guided Content Adaptation. The authors evaluated PAGE through an expert review and a user study where students used an intelligent tutoring system enhanced by PAGE. Results indicated that personalized content led to improved learning outcomes, higher engagement, increased perceived relevance, and greater trust compared to standardized materials. The paper concludes that LLM-powered personalization can be valuable in education, emphasizing the importance of narrative coherence, factual trustworthiness, and careful selection of foundation models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Addresses a Real Problem:** The paper tackles a significant and persistent challenge in education: the disconnect between standardized content and individual student needs. This problem is well-established in the educational literature.
    *   **Novel Framework:** PAGE provides a comprehensive framework for personalized content generation, going beyond superficial adaptations. The integration of student profiling, knowledge retrieval, and pedagogical guidelines is well-structured.
    *   **Rigorous Evaluation:** The two-stage evaluation process (expert review and user study) strengthens the paper's findings. The expert review validates the pedagogical quality of the generated content, while the user study provides insights into the real-world impact.  The experimental design in the user study is sound, with clearly defined conditions and data collection methods.
    *   **Clear Results and Interpretations:** The results are clearly presented and interpreted, with appropriate statistical analyses. The qualitative data adds depth to the quantitative findings, providing a richer understanding of the students' experiences.
    *   **Identifies Key Design Implications:** The paper effectively distills key design implications for creating trustworthy and context-aware learning experiences, such as the crucial role of retrieval-augmentation for factual correctness and strong reasoning of base models to synthesize retrieved knowledge.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope:** The evaluation is conducted within a relatively narrow context (two introductory university courses). The generalizability to more advanced or specialized subjects may be limited.
    *   **Short-Term Evaluation:** The user study assesses the immediate impact of personalization. The long-term effects on knowledge retention and sustained engagement are not investigated.  The authors acknowledge this limitation.
    *   **Sample Size and Diversity:** While the sample size of 40 is reasonable, the authors acknowledge the limited demographic diversity. The lack of specific representation from underrepresented groups is a concern.
    *   **Reliance on LLMs and Potential Biases:** The framework depends on LLMs, which can inherit and amplify societal biases.  While the authors acknowledge this issue and implement mitigation strategies, the potential for biased content generation remains a concern. However, the mitigation strategie such as the rule-based checks that filters out contents for overly brief, elementary and introductory is one of the highlight of this paper.
    *   **Novelty vs. Incremental Improvement:** While the PAGE framework is well-designed, the novelty might be incremental. The components of student profiling, knowledge retrieval, and LLM-based content generation are not entirely new individually. However, the integration and specific implementation within an educational context contribute to the paper's value.

*   **Significance:**
    *   The paper provides a practical demonstration of how LLMs can be used to personalize education at scale.  This is particularly relevant given the increasing accessibility and power of LLMs.
    *   The framework and design principles outlined in the paper can inform the development of future personalized learning systems.
    *   The emphasis on pedagogical soundness and ethical considerations is commendable.  The paper promotes a responsible and student-centered approach to LLM-powered education.
    *   The paper builds on the limitations of prior works. The previous studies focuses on superficial personalisation or task-specific application, however this paper focuses on deep contextual adaptation in the setting of education.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of **7.5** to this paper. The PAGE framework is well-designed and addresses a significant problem in education. The evaluation is rigorous, and the results demonstrate the potential of LLM-powered personalization. The paper also identifies key design implications for future research and development.

However, the limitations related to scope, sample size, long-term effects, and potential biases prevent a higher score. While the framework is comprehensive, the novelty may be incremental rather than revolutionary. The contributions are substantial but not groundbreaking enough to warrant a score in the higher range. This is a solid piece of work that contributes meaningfully to the field, and will likely be cited by others working on LLM-driven educational tools.

### Forecasting and Visualizing Air Quality from Sky Images with Vision-Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15076v1)
- **Authors**: Mohammad Saleh Vahdatpour, Maryam Eyvazi, Yanqing Zhang
- **Abstract**: Air pollution remains a critical threat to public health and environmental sustainability, yet conventional monitoring systems are often constrained by limited spatial coverage and accessibility. This paper proposes an AI-driven agent that predicts ambient air pollution levels from sky images and synthesizes realistic visualizations of pollution scenarios using generative modeling. Our approach combines statistical texture analysis with supervised learning for pollution classification, and leverages vision-language model (VLM)-guided image generation to produce interpretable representations of air quality conditions. The generated visuals simulate varying degrees of pollution, offering a foundation for user-facing interfaces that improve transparency and support informed environmental decision-making. These outputs can be seamlessly integrated into intelligent applications aimed at enhancing situational awareness and encouraging behavioral responses based on real-time forecasts. We validate our method using a dataset of urban sky images and demonstrate its effectiveness in both pollution level estimation and semantically consistent visual synthesis. The system design further incorporates human-centered user experience principles to ensure accessibility, clarity, and public engagement in air quality forecasting. To support scalable and energy-efficient deployment, future iterations will incorporate a green CNN architecture enhanced with FPGA-based incremental learning, enabling real-time inference on edge platforms.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents an AI-driven system that forecasts and visualizes air quality using sky images. The system combines statistical texture analysis and supervised learning to classify pollution levels. It then uses a vision-language model (VLM) to generate realistic visualizations of different pollution scenarios, allowing users to understand the predicted air quality more intuitively.  The system aims to improve transparency, support informed decision-making, and enable behavioral responses based on real-time forecasts, particularly in areas with limited conventional monitoring infrastructure.  The authors evaluate their system on a curated sky image dataset, demonstrating its effectiveness in pollution level estimation and visual synthesis.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty of this paper lies in its integration of several existing techniques into a cohesive system for air quality visualization. While the individual components like CNN-based classification, Gabor filter analysis, and VLM-guided image generation are not entirely new, their synergistic application in this specific context, especially the VLM-controlled generative aspect tied to pollution classification, represents a valuable contribution. The idea of using sky images for air quality estimation is also not novel, the addition of visual narratives via generative models grounded in air quality classifications enhance understandability, which sets it apart from previous works. However, the paper does not introduce substantial theoretical breakthroughs or fundamentally new algorithms.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses an important and pressing issue: air pollution monitoring and communication.  The system has the potential to improve public awareness and enable more informed decision-making related to environmental health. The emphasis on a user-centered design and lightweight architecture for potential deployment on edge platforms enhances the practical relevance of the work.  The focus on interpretability is also a significant strength, as many existing air quality forecasting systems are "black boxes." The system promotes accessibility by translating numerical pollution data into visuals that are easier to interpret by general public.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Integration of multiple techniques:** Combines traditional statistical methods, deep learning, and VLMs effectively.
    *   **Focus on interpretability:** Prioritizes visual explanation and user understanding.
    *   **User-centered design:** Incorporates user feedback to create an accessible interface.
    *   **Potential for real-world impact:**  Addresses a critical issue and aims for practical deployment.
    *   **Clear and well-structured presentation:** The paper is well-written and easy to follow.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited novelty in individual components:** Relies on established techniques rather than introducing fundamentally new algorithms.
    *   **Evaluation could be more extensive:**  While the initial results are promising, more rigorous evaluation in diverse real-world settings is needed. A comparison against simpler baselines that combine air quality classification with simpler visualisations should also be considered.
    *   **Lack of discussion on limitations:** The paper could benefit from a more thorough discussion of the system's limitations, such as its sensitivity to specific weather conditions (e.g., heavy rain, fog) that might impede clear sky image capture and, thus, classification accuracy.
    *   **Scalability:** The integration of visually consistent air quality visualization can lead to a costly scalability of the training samples.
    *   **Energy Consumption:** The VLM models are energy consuming. While authors mention the future plan for green CNN, they can discuss some possible solutions for reducing the energy consumption of VLM models.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper has the potential to influence the design of future air quality monitoring systems, particularly those aimed at public engagement and education. The combination of forecasting and visualization could serve as a model for other environmental monitoring applications.

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a solid application of existing techniques to an important problem. The integration of VLMs for visual synthesis is a novel and valuable contribution that enhances the interpretability of air quality forecasts. While the paper does not introduce major theoretical breakthroughs, its practical focus and emphasis on user understanding make it a worthwhile contribution to the field of environmental informatics. The weaknesses, particularly the limited novelty in individual components and need for more extensive evaluation, prevent it from achieving a higher score.

### Adaptive LoRA Experts Allocation and Selection for Federated Fine-Tuning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15087v1)
- **Authors**: Lei Wang, Jieming Bian, Letian Zhang, Jie Xu
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have demonstrated impressive capabilities across various tasks, but fine-tuning them for domain-specific applications often requires substantial domain-specific data that may be distributed across multiple organizations. Federated Learning (FL) offers a privacy-preserving solution, but faces challenges with computational constraints when applied to LLMs. Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA) has emerged as a parameter-efficient fine-tuning approach, though a single LoRA module often struggles with heterogeneous data across diverse domains. This paper addresses two critical challenges in federated LoRA fine-tuning: 1. determining the optimal number and allocation of LoRA experts across heterogeneous clients, and 2. enabling clients to selectively utilize these experts based on their specific data characteristics. We propose FedLEASE (Federated adaptive LoRA Expert Allocation and SElection), a novel framework that adaptively clusters clients based on representation similarity to allocate and train domain-specific LoRA experts. It also introduces an adaptive top-$M$ Mixture-of-Experts mechanism that allows each client to select the optimal number of utilized experts. Our extensive experiments on diverse benchmark datasets demonstrate that FedLEASE significantly outperforms existing federated fine-tuning approaches in heterogeneous client settings while maintaining communication efficiency.
- **Summary**: This paper proposes FedLEASE, a novel federated learning framework for fine-tuning large language models (LLMs) using Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA). The framework addresses two key challenges: (1) determining the optimal number and allocation of LoRA experts across heterogeneous clients, and (2) enabling clients to selectively utilize these experts based on their data characteristics. FedLEASE adaptively clusters clients based on LoRA parameter similarity to allocate and train domain-specific LoRA experts, and introduces an adaptive top-M Mixture-of-Experts mechanism for clients to select the optimal number of utilized experts. The paper presents extensive experiments on diverse benchmark datasets, demonstrating that FedLEASE outperforms existing federated fine-tuning approaches in heterogeneous client settings while maintaining communication efficiency.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the combination of client clustering based on LoRA parameter similarity and an adaptive top-M MoE mechanism within a federated learning framework. While LoRA and MoE have been used in centralized settings, their application with data-driven client clustering and adaptive expert selection in FL is a relatively new approach. Existing FL methods often rely on a single shared LoRA module or binary global-local designs, which cannot capture the nuances of client similarities and expert needs. The proposed adaptive top-M mechanism is more flexible compared to traditional MoE and addresses the limitation of requiring manual specification of the fixed number of experts for all inputs.

**Significance:** The paper addresses a critical problem in federated learning: effectively fine-tuning LLMs in heterogeneous environments with limited communication and computational resources. The proposed FedLEASE framework offers a potential solution to this problem by adaptively allocating and selecting LoRA experts based on client-specific data characteristics. The experimental results demonstrate that FedLEASE significantly outperforms existing methods, suggesting that it has the potential to improve the performance of federated LLMs in real-world applications.

**Strengths:**
*   **Problem Formulation:** Clearly defines and motivates the challenges in federated LoRA fine-tuning: expert allocation and selection.
*   **Technical Contribution:** The combination of client clustering based on LoRA similarity and adaptive top-M MoE is a novel and potentially effective approach.
*   **Empirical Evaluation:** Extensive experiments on diverse datasets (GLUE and FLAN) demonstrate the effectiveness of FedLEASE.
*   **Ablation Studies:** Ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of each component of FedLEASE (expert allocation and adaptive top-M).
*   **Convergence Analysis:** It has convergence analysis that shows convergence under certain assumptions.

**Weaknesses:**
*   **Assumption E.4 (Cluster Assignment Stability):** In many practical scenarios, it's unrealistic to assume that the cluster assignments remain stable. The framework has to re-cluster dynamically to reflect the changes in the data distribution.
*   **Complexity of the adaptive top-M mechanism**: The adaptive routing mechanism expands the output space which may add complexity to practical deployment.
*   **Scalability**: The experimental setup involves a limited number of clients (16, 32). While the paper analyzes client numbers, there isn’t enough experimental evidence to conclude about its scalability. The clustering has O(N^2) complexity which can cause trouble if N is big.
*   **Limited Baseline Comparison:** It only compares against a few federated LoRA fine-tuning methods, but lacks comparison with other federated learning algorithms specifically suited for heterogeneous data.
*   **Parameter sensitivity:** The method needs hyperparameter tuning for the clustering stage, e.g. the M_max is tuned based on silhouette scores.

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of federated learning for LLMs. It addresses a practical problem with a novel and well-evaluated approach. The combination of client clustering and adaptive expert selection offers a more flexible and effective solution compared to existing methods. However, the limitations regarding the assumption of stable cluster assignments, limited scalability analysis, the parameter sensitivity, and the lack of convergence guarantees reduce its potential impact. The limited scope of client numbers and the stable clustering assumption prevent the framework from being a universal solution for all possible use cases in federated learning.

Score: 6.5

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### LLM-OREF: An Open Relation Extraction Framework Based on Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15089v1)
- **Authors**: Hongyao Tu, Liang Zhang, Yujie Lin, Xin Lin, Haibo Zhang, Long Zhang, Jinsong Su
- **Abstract**: The goal of open relation extraction (OpenRE) is to develop an RE model that can generalize to new relations not encountered during training. Existing studies primarily formulate OpenRE as a clustering task. They first cluster all test instances based on the similarity between the instances, and then manually assign a new relation to each cluster. However, their reliance on human annotation limits their practicality. In this paper, we propose an OpenRE framework based on large language models (LLMs), which directly predicts new relations for test instances by leveraging their strong language understanding and generation abilities, without human intervention. Specifically, our framework consists of two core components: (1) a relation discoverer (RD), designed to predict new relations for test instances based on \textit{demonstrations} formed by training instances with known relations; and (2) a relation predictor (RP), used to select the most likely relation for a test instance from $n$ candidate relations, guided by \textit{demonstrations} composed of their instances. To enhance the ability of our framework to predict new relations, we design a self-correcting inference strategy composed of three stages: relation discovery, relation denoising, and relation prediction. In the first stage, we use RD to preliminarily predict new relations for all test instances. Next, we apply RP to select some high-reliability test instances for each new relation from the prediction results of RD through a cross-validation method. During the third stage, we employ RP to re-predict the relations of all test instances based on the demonstrations constructed from these reliable test instances. Extensive experiments on three OpenRE datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of our framework. We release our code at https://github.com/XMUDeepLIT/LLM-OREF.git.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes "LLM-OREF," a framework for Open Relation Extraction (OpenRE) using Large Language Models (LLMs). The goal is to discover and predict new relations in text without relying on human annotation. The framework has two components: a Relation Discoverer (RD) that predicts new relations based on demonstrations built from training data with known relations, and a Relation Predictor (RP) that selects the most likely relation from a set of candidates, guided by similar demonstrations. A self-correcting inference strategy with three stages (relation discovery, relation denoising, and relation prediction) is introduced to enhance prediction accuracy. Experimental results on three OpenRE datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of the framework.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in directly applying LLMs to the OpenRE task in a manner that does not rely on human annotation, addressing a key limitation of prior work. The use of a two-component architecture (RD and RP) with a self-correcting inference strategy is a reasonable design choice to balance exploration of new relations with accurate prediction. The distillation loss to enhance RD training is also a valuable contribution.

*   **Significance:** OpenRE is a challenging and important task as it aims to overcome the limitations of traditional RE models by allowing the discovery of new relations. The proposed framework has the potential to automate the discovery of new relations, which has valuable applications in various downstream tasks. The empirical results on the tested datasets are strong, showing improvements over existing methods. The ablation studies provide some insight into the importance of each component of the framework.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The framework addresses a practical limitation of existing OpenRE methods (reliance on human annotation).
    *   The two-component design and self-correcting inference strategy are well-motivated and contribute to the framework's effectiveness.
    *   The use of distillation loss for RD training is a smart way to leverage RP's higher accuracy.
    *   The experimental results demonstrate consistent improvement over baseline models on three benchmark datasets.
    *   The ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of each component.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   While the components are reasonably designed, the methods themselves aren't drastically novel. They represent an incremental advancement in applying LLMs to OpenRE.

    *   The limitation section could provide a more in-depth exploration of the weaknesses of the approach. For example, the dependence on the quality of demonstrations could be further discussed.
    *   The choice of hyperparameters could be better explained, even if included only in the appendix. The justification of K=3 is weak since it mentions the pass@K improves with increasing predictions and that this enabled a more effective denoising which are both contradicting justifications.

    * While the paper does address the lack of reliance on human annotations which is a step further than previous works, it still relies on *some* level of supervision given that existing labeled data is still needed to train both the RD and RP components. The paper doesn't explore the impact of the quality, quantity or distribution of the labeled data on performance.
    * The analysis comparing different distillation loss weights provides a weak reasoning based on achieving the 'best performance'. A more detailed reasoning is needed to provide an actual explanation.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper can influence future research in OpenRE by demonstrating the effectiveness of directly leveraging LLMs for new relation discovery. It also encourages the development of methods for improving the accuracy and efficiency of LLMs in this domain. However, the relative novelty is incremental. The community is already exploring LLMs so a more advanced contribution may be needed to stand out.

**Score: 6.5**

**Justification:**

The paper presents a well-engineered OpenRE framework that improves upon existing methods by directly leveraging LLMs without human annotation. It achieves strong experimental results and provides valuable insights through ablation studies. However, the overall novelty is incremental rather than groundbreaking. The paper lacks a deep analysis of the limitations and relies on heuristics for some design choices. Therefore, while it is a solid contribution to the field, it does not warrant a higher score. The potential impact on the field is medium, primarily influencing future research directions in applying LLMs to OpenRE.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### The Energy-Efficient Hierarchical Neural Network with Fast FPGA-Based Incremental Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15097v1)
- **Authors**: Mohammad Saleh Vahdatpour, Huaiyuan Chu, Yanqing Zhang
- **Abstract**: The rising computational and energy demands of deep learning, particularly in large-scale architectures such as foundation models and large language models (LLMs), pose significant challenges to sustainability. Traditional gradient-based training methods are inefficient, requiring numerous iterative updates and high power consumption. To address these limitations, we propose a hybrid framework that combines hierarchical decomposition with FPGA-based direct equation solving and incremental learning. Our method divides the neural network into two functional tiers: lower layers are optimized via single-step equation solving on FPGAs for efficient and parallelizable feature extraction, while higher layers employ adaptive incremental learning to support continual updates without full retraining. Building upon this foundation, we introduce the Compound LLM framework, which explicitly deploys LLM modules across both hierarchy levels. The lower-level LLM handles reusable representation learning with minimal energy overhead, while the upper-level LLM performs adaptive decision-making through energy-aware updates. This integrated design enhances scalability, reduces redundant computation, and aligns with the principles of sustainable AI. Theoretical analysis and architectural insights demonstrate that our method reduces computational costs significantly while preserving high model performance, making it well-suited for edge deployment and real-time adaptation in energy-constrained environments.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a hybrid framework for energy-efficient AI training, particularly focusing on Large Language Models (LLMs). The core idea is to combine hierarchical decomposition with FPGA-based direct equation solving and incremental learning.  The neural network is divided into two functional tiers: lower layers responsible for feature extraction are optimized using FPGA-accelerated direct equation solving for efficiency, while higher layers responsible for decision-making are trained incrementally to adapt to new data without full retraining. The paper introduces the Compound LLM architecture, explicitly deploying LLM modules at both hierarchical levels. The lower-level LLM handles representation learning, and the upper-level LLM performs adaptive decision-making. The authors claim this approach enhances scalability, reduces redundant computation, and promotes sustainable AI practices.  The paper includes theoretical analysis and architectural insights to support the claim that this method reduces computational costs and preserves performance, making it suitable for edge deployment.

**Critical Evaluation:**
