### Conditional Prior-based Non-stationary Channel Estimation Using Accelerated Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15182v1)
- **Authors**: Muhammad Ahmed Mohsin, Ahsan Bilal, Muhammad Umer, Asad Aali, Muhammad Ali Jamshed, Dean F. Hougen, John M. Cioffi
- **Abstract**: Wireless channels in motion-rich urban microcell (UMi) settings are non-stationary; mobility and scatterer dynamics shift the distribution over time, degrading classical and deep estimators. This work proposes conditional prior diffusion for channel estimation, which learns a history-conditioned score to denoise noisy channel snapshots. A temporal encoder with cross-time attention compresses a short observation window into a context vector, which captures the channel's instantaneous coherence and steers the denoiser via feature-wise modulation. In inference, an SNR-matched initialization selects the diffusion step whose marginal aligns with the measured input SNR, and the process follows a shortened, geometrically spaced schedule, preserving the signal-to-noise trajectory with far fewer iterations. Temporal self-conditioning with the previous channel estimate and a training-only smoothness penalty further stabilizes evolution without biasing the test-time estimator. Evaluations on a 3GPP benchmark show lower NMSE across all SNRs than LMMSE, GMM, LSTM, and LDAMP baselines, demonstrating stable performance and strong high SNR fidelity.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of channel estimation in non-stationary wireless environments, specifically focusing on urban microcells (UMi) where user mobility introduces time-varying channel characteristics. It proposes a conditional prior-based diffusion model that learns a history-conditioned prior from a causal window of channel observations to denoise noisy channel snapshots. The model incorporates a temporal encoder (ConvLSTM with cross-time attention), SNR-matched initialization and geometric spacing to accelerate convergence. The approach also employs Gauss-Markov self-conditioning and temporal smoothness regularization to enhance temporal stability. The paper demonstrates the model's superior performance on a 3GPP UMi benchmark compared to various baseline methods, including LMMSE, GMM, LSTM, and LDAMP, showcasing stable performance and high SNR fidelity.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the integration of several established techniques into a cohesive framework tailored for non-stationary channel estimation. While diffusion models have been applied to channel estimation before, the specific combination of conditional priors learned from temporal context using ConvLSTM-attention, SNR-matched initialization, geometrically spaced sampling, and temporal self-conditioning appears to be a novel contribution. The use of FiLM for conditioning is also a relatively recent advance that is well deployed here.

* **Significance:** The problem of channel estimation in dynamic wireless environments is of paramount importance for modern wireless systems. The proposed method addresses the limitations of traditional and deep learning-based estimators that struggle with non-stationarity. A robust and accurate channel estimator leads directly to better spectral efficiency, higher data rates, and improved user experience, making this work significant. The experimental results demonstrate a considerable improvement over standard techniques, suggesting that the proposed approach is a step forward. However, the simulation environment simplifies real-world complexities, impacting the generalization of the results.

* **Strengths:**
    *   The paper clearly articulates the problem of non-stationary channel estimation and provides a comprehensive solution leveraging diffusion models and temporal priors.
    *   The proposed model integrates several established techniques (ConvLSTM, attention, SNR matching, temporal conditioning) effectively.
    *   The experimental results convincingly demonstrate superior performance compared to several baseline methods.
    *   The paper is well-written and provides sufficient technical detail.
    *  The ablation studies on the various modules would further enhance the paper.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * The 3GPP UMi benchmark is somewhat controlled and simplified. While it captures the acceleration component it is a simplified simulation.
    * The computational complexity of diffusion models can be high, which is addressed by the proposed acceleration strategy. However, a detailed comparison of computational costs with other methods is missing.
    * The paper could benefit from a more detailed analysis of the sensitivity of the model to different hyperparameters, such as the diffusion step schedule and the temporal window length.

* **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence future research on channel estimation, especially in dynamic wireless environments. The proposed approach could serve as a foundation for further development of more robust and adaptive channel estimators. If the proposed method generalizes to real-world scenarios (which isn't demonstrated), it has the potential to be incorporated into future wireless communication systems.

**Score:** 7

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel combination of existing techniques to address a significant problem in wireless communications. While the contributions themselves are not groundbreaking individually, their integration is novel and demonstrably improves performance. The primary weakness is the lack of real-world validation and comprehensive computational cost analysis. Although the improvement over baselines is convincing in the controlled simulation, further testing and optimization are necessary to translate the performance into an actual wireless implementation. Furthermore, a formal analysis of the computational tradeoffs would add valuable information regarding the practical utility of the approach. The paper's potential to impact research is high due to the relevance and difficulty of the tackled problem. The current simulation environment somewhat limits the overall score, but the ideas presented here are solid.

- **Classification**: cs.DC
- **Score**: 7/10

### Understand Before You Generate: Self-Guided Training for Autoregressive Image Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15185v1)
- **Authors**: Xiaoyu Yue, Zidong Wang, Yuqing Wang, Wenlong Zhang, Xihui Liu, Wanli Ouyang, Lei Bai, Luping Zhou
- **Abstract**: Recent studies have demonstrated the importance of high-quality visual representations in image generation and have highlighted the limitations of generative models in image understanding. As a generative paradigm originally designed for natural language, autoregressive models face similar challenges. In this work, we present the first systematic investigation into the mechanisms of applying the next-token prediction paradigm to the visual domain. We identify three key properties that hinder the learning of high-level visual semantics: local and conditional dependence, inter-step semantic inconsistency, and spatial invariance deficiency. We show that these issues can be effectively addressed by introducing self-supervised objectives during training, leading to a novel training framework, Self-guided Training for AutoRegressive models (ST-AR). Without relying on pre-trained representation models, ST-AR significantly enhances the image understanding ability of autoregressive models and leads to improved generation quality. Specifically, ST-AR brings approximately 42% FID improvement for LlamaGen-L and 49% FID improvement for LlamaGen-XL, while maintaining the same sampling strategy.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've reviewed the provided paper and will provide a summary, followed by a critical evaluation of its novelty and significance.

**Summary**

The paper "Understand Before You Generate: Self-Guided Training for Autoregressive Image Generation" investigates the challenges of applying autoregressive models, originally designed for natural language processing, to image generation. The authors identify three key properties that hinder the learning of high-level visual semantics: local and conditional dependence, inter-step semantic inconsistency, and spatial invariance deficiency. To address these issues, they propose a novel training framework called Self-guided Training for AutoRegressive models (ST-AR). ST-AR integrates self-supervised objectives, including masked image modeling and contrastive learning, into the next-token prediction paradigm. Experiments demonstrate that ST-AR significantly enhances the image understanding ability of autoregressive models, leading to improved generation quality without relying on pre-trained representation models.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:**

    *   **Strengths:** The paper systematically identifies and articulates specific challenges faced when adapting autoregressive models from language to image generation. The three identified problems – local dependence, semantic inconsistency, and spatial invariance – provide a clear framework for understanding limitations. The proposed ST-AR framework is a novel combination of techniques, specifically tailored to address the identified issues in AR image generation. Random masking on attention maps is an interesting trick to increase the attention distance.
    *   **Weaknesses:** While the components of ST-AR (masked image modeling and contrastive learning) are not entirely new, their combination and application to autoregressive image generation is novel, but relies on standard techniques. The idea to use contrastive learning between time steps and different views is a natural next step, making the novelty somewhat incremental. The novelty lies in specific implementation and application.

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Strengths:** The improvements in FID score and linear probing accuracy show that the proposed method works effectively and improves image quality. Achieving significant performance gains without pre-trained models is highly valuable. The paper addresses a relevant and crucial problem in the field. The paper shows a comparable result compared with larger models with 4x parameters, which highlights the significance.
    *   **Weaknesses:** The paper relies on established evaluation metrics (FID, IS, linear probing). Although these are standard, it is important to consider the well-known limitations of these metrics. Furthermore, the improvements, while significant numerically, could be subjectively assessed. Visual examples would strengthen the impact.

*   **Clarity and Reproducibility:**

    *   **Strengths:** The paper's approach is well-motivated, and the explanation of the method is clear. The authors provide sufficient implementation details.
    *   **Weaknesses:** Some implementation details could be further clarified.

*   **Impact and Potential Influence:**
    * The paper paves the way to leverage self-supervised learning techniques for autoregressive image generation, showing that this direction is promising.

*   **Overall:**

    The paper offers a systematic analysis of challenges and a targeted solution (ST-AR) that improves image quality. It shows that with some self-supervised training methods, autoregressive models are more capable. The use of visual examples is also sufficient. The novelty is not groundbreaking, but the approach and results are significant.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:** The paper is a solid contribution with clear improvements. The systematic analysis, and tailored solution add merit. The technical novelty is incremental as the techniques are relatively well-known, but the application is valuable. Overall, it's a strong, well-executed paper that contributes meaningfully to the field of autoregressive image generation.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Fast and Fluent Diffusion Language Models via Convolutional Decoding and Rejective Fine-tuning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15188v1)
- **Authors**: Yeongbin Seo, Dongha Lee, Jaehyung Kim, Jinyoung Yeo
- **Abstract**: Autoregressive (AR) language models generate text one token at a time, which limits their inference speed. Diffusion-based language models offer a promising alternative, as they can decode multiple tokens in parallel. However, we identify a key bottleneck in current diffusion LMs: the long decoding-window problem, where tokens generated far from the input context often become irrelevant or repetitive. Previous solutions like semi-autoregressive address this issue by splitting windows into blocks, but this sacrifices speed and bidirectionality, eliminating the main advantage of diffusion models. To overcome this, we propose Convolutional decoding (Conv), a normalization-based method that narrows the decoding window without hard segmentation, leading to better fluency and flexibility. Additionally, we introduce Rejecting Rule-based Fine-Tuning (R2FT), a post-hoc training scheme that better aligns tokens at positions far from context. Our methods achieve state-of-the-art results on open-ended generation benchmarks (e.g., AlpacaEval) among diffusion LM baselines, with significantly lower step size than previous works, demonstrating both speed and quality improvements.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary**

The paper addresses a key challenge in diffusion-based language models (LMs): the "long decoding-window" (LDW) problem. This problem arises because diffusion LMs can generate multiple tokens in parallel, but tokens far from the input context tend to be less relevant and more random. The authors propose two methods to mitigate this:

1.  **Convolutional Decoding (Conv):**  This method narrows the decoding window using normalization instead of hard segmentation, improving fluency.

2.  **Rejecting Rule-based Fine-Tuning (R2FT):**  This post-hoc training scheme aligns distant tokens with the context by rejecting rule-based synthesized negative behaviors (corrupted version).

The paper demonstrates that combining these methods achieves state-of-the-art results among diffusion LM baselines on open-ended generation benchmarks. They also address the limitations of semi-autoregressive approaches, showing that their methods retain speed and flexibility.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces two novel techniques, Conv and R2FT, to improve the fluency and coherence of diffusion LMs. The identification and explicit framing of the LDW problem are also novel and contribute to a deeper understanding of the challenges of diffusion LMs. The analysis of limitations in semi-AR approaches is a valuable addition.

*   **Significance:** The paper's contributions are significant for several reasons:
    *   **Addressing a core problem:** LDW is a genuine bottleneck in the development of more practical and higher-quality diffusion LMs.
    *   **Improved performance:**  The results demonstrate significant improvements in open-ended generation tasks, a domain where previous diffusion LMs have struggled.
    *   **Speed and Quality:** By avoiding the issues that hurt semi-AR approaches, the methods allow for a better trade-off between speed and quality. The demonstrated reduction in step size while maintaining or improving quality shows promise for faster inference.
    *   **Practicality:** The methods are relatively simple to implement. Conv involves adding a normalization step, and R2FT uses a post-hoc training scheme with rule-based data augmentation.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the LDW problem and its causes.
    *   **Well-Designed Methods:** Conv and R2FT are well-motivated and designed to address specific aspects of the LDW problem.
    *   **Comprehensive Experiments:** The paper provides a thorough experimental evaluation on multiple benchmarks, comparing against relevant baselines. There is also a good amount of ablation.
    *   **Thorough analysis:** the authors analyze each component in detail, offering good justification of their method.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope of Bidirectional Evaluation:** While the paper mentions bidirectionality as an advantage of diffusion LMs, the experimental evaluation focuses primarily on unidirectional generation. Future work is needed to demonstrate the benefits of the Conv and R2FT in true bidirectional settings and for bidirectional-specific tasks.
    *   **Rule-based corruption dependency:** Rule-based corruption requires careful selection of which rules to be used, otherwise the performance may be compromised
    *   **Some technical depth is missing:** Although there is a theoretical guarantee on structural coherence improvement, it may lack some details.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence the development of more practical and high-performing diffusion LMs. The identified LDW problem and proposed solutions can guide future research in this area. By enabling faster and more coherent generation, the paper could contribute to broader adoption of diffusion LMs for various applications.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:**

The paper is a solid contribution to the field. It identifies a significant problem in diffusion LMs, proposes well-designed and effective solutions, and provides a thorough experimental evaluation. It's not a groundbreaking paradigm shift, but the LDW problem the authors address is a very important problem in the field, hence, the proposed method offers a noticeable improvement. The paper's practicality and potential for impact are high. The limitations regarding true bidirectional evaluation keep it from scoring higher.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Evolving Language Models without Labels: Majority Drives Selection, Novelty Promotes Variation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15194v1)
- **Authors**: Yujun Zhou, Zhenwen Liang, Haolin Liu, Wenhao Yu, Kishan Panaganti, Linfeng Song, Dian Yu, Xiangliang Zhang, Haitao Mi, Dong Yu
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly trained with reinforcement learning from verifiable rewards (RLVR), yet real-world deployment demands models that can self-improve without labels or external judges. Existing label-free methods, confidence minimization, self-consistency, or majority-vote objectives, stabilize learning but steadily shrink exploration, causing an entropy collapse: generations become shorter, less diverse, and brittle. Unlike prior approaches such as Test-Time Reinforcement Learning (TTRL), which primarily adapt models to the immediate unlabeled dataset at hand, our goal is broader: to enable general improvements without sacrificing the model's inherent exploration capacity and generalization ability, i.e., evolving. We formalize this issue and propose EVolution-Oriented and Label-free Reinforcement Learning (EVOL-RL), a simple rule that couples stability with variation under a label-free setting. EVOL-RL keeps the majority-voted answer as a stable anchor (selection) while adding a novelty-aware reward that favors responses whose reasoning differs from what has already been produced (variation), measured in semantic space. Implemented with GRPO, EVOL-RL also uses asymmetric clipping to preserve strong signals and an entropy regularizer to sustain search. This majority-for-selection + novelty-for-variation design prevents collapse, maintains longer and more informative chains of thought, and improves both pass@1 and pass@n. EVOL-RL consistently outperforms the majority-only TTRL baseline; e.g., training on label-free AIME24 lifts Qwen3-4B-Base AIME25 pass@1 from TTRL's 4.6% to 16.4%, and pass@16 from 18.5% to 37.9%. EVOL-RL not only prevents diversity collapse but also unlocks stronger generalization across domains (e.g., GPQA). Furthermore, we demonstrate that EVOL-RL also boosts performance in the RLVR setting, highlighting its broad applicability.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of "entropy collapse" in label-free reinforcement learning (RL) for large language models (LLMs). Existing label-free methods that rely on self-consistency or majority voting tend to stabilize learning but reduce exploration, leading to shorter, less diverse, and more brittle generations. The paper proposes EVOL-RL (EVolution-Oriented Label-free Reinforcement Learning), which combines majority voting for stability (selection) with a novelty-aware reward that favors responses with semantically different reasoning (variation).  EVOL-RL also uses asymmetric clipping to preserve strong signals and an entropy regularizer to sustain search. The authors demonstrate that EVOL-RL prevents collapse, maintains longer and more informative chains of thought, and improves performance across several math reasoning benchmarks, including improved generalization to out-of-domain datasets.  They also show performance gains in the standard RLVR setting.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies and formalizes a significant problem in label-free LLM training: entropy collapse. This is a practical concern hindering the development of autonomous LLMs.
    *   **Well-Motivated Approach:** The connection to evolutionary principles (variation + selection) provides a strong and intuitive motivation for the proposed EVOL-RL method.
    *   **Simple and Elegant Solution:** The "majority + novelty" reward design is conceptually simple and relatively easy to implement.  The asymmetric clipping and entropy regularization enhance the core idea effectively.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The paper provides compelling empirical evidence that EVOL-RL outperforms majority-only baselines like TTRL on multiple math reasoning benchmarks, including significant improvements in out-of-domain generalization. The experiments cover different model scales and training data sizes, demonstrating robustness. Ablation studies effectively show the contribution of each component. The analysis of training dynamics in Figure 3 provide compelling evidence.
    *   **Code Release:** Making the code available promotes reproducibility and further research.
    *   **Demonstrated improvement in RLVR Setting:** The demonstration that EVOL-RL improves performance in the RLVR setting broadens its potential applications.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Domain:** The experiments are primarily focused on mathematical reasoning. While this is a challenging domain, it would be valuable to see if EVOL-RL generalizes to other types of tasks, such as natural language generation, summarization, or dialogue.
    *   **Computational Cost:** The approach involves generating multiple responses per prompt and computing semantic similarity. This can be computationally expensive, especially for large models and long sequences. The paper could benefit from a discussion of the computational overhead and potential optimizations.
    *   **Reliance on Embedding Model:** The novelty reward relies on the quality of the embedding model used to measure semantic similarity. The choice of embedding model and its potential impact on the results could be discussed more thoroughly.
    *   **Limited novelty in optimization technique:** They use GRPO, but GRPO is a relatively recent technique. GRPO may not be the "best" technique to use here, or there may be other alternatives that need to be evaluated.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the EVOL-RL framework, which combines majority voting with novelty-aware reward to address the entropy collapse problem in label-free LLM training. The explicit connection to evolutionary principles and the specific design of the novelty reward are novel contributions.
    *   **Significance:** The paper addresses a practical challenge hindering the development of autonomous LLMs. The EVOL-RL framework offers a promising approach for improving the stability, diversity, and generalization ability of LLMs trained without labels. The strong empirical results and code release suggest that EVOL-RL could have a significant impact on the field.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper has some limitations, its strengths outweigh its weaknesses. The paper clearly identifies and addresses a significant problem, provides a well-motivated and elegant solution, and presents compelling empirical evidence. The novelty of the approach, the strong results, and the code release suggest that EVOL-RL could have a lasting impact on the field. The limitations concerning limited domain, computational cost and reliance on embedding models slightly reduce the paper's overall impact. The optimization algorithm might also need to be evaluated more.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### Beyond Surface Alignment: Rebuilding LLMs Safety Mechanism via Probabilistically Ablating Refusal Direction
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15202v1)
- **Authors**: Yuanbo Xie, Yingjie Zhang, Tianyun Liu, Duohe Ma, Tingwen Liu
- **Abstract**: Jailbreak attacks pose persistent threats to large language models (LLMs). Current safety alignment methods have attempted to address these issues, but they experience two significant limitations: insufficient safety alignment depth and unrobust internal defense mechanisms. These limitations make them vulnerable to adversarial attacks such as prefilling and refusal direction manipulation. We introduce DeepRefusal, a robust safety alignment framework that overcomes these issues. DeepRefusal forces the model to dynamically rebuild its refusal mechanisms from jailbreak states. This is achieved by probabilistically ablating the refusal direction across layers and token depths during fine-tuning. Our method not only defends against prefilling and refusal direction attacks but also demonstrates strong resilience against other unseen jailbreak strategies. Extensive evaluations on four open-source LLM families and six representative attacks show that DeepRefusal reduces attack success rates by approximately 95%, while maintaining model capabilities with minimal performance degradation.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Beyond Surface Alignment: Rebuilding LLMs Safety Mechanism via Probabilistically Ablating Refusal Direction" introduces DeepRefusal, a novel framework designed to improve the robustness of Large Language Models (LLMs) against jailbreak attacks. The core idea behind DeepRefusal is to address the limitations of existing safety alignment methods, which the authors argue are too focused on surface-level token manipulation. DeepRefusal achieves this by simulating jailbreak scenarios internally, at the representation level, by probabilistically ablating the refusal direction across different layers and token depths during fine-tuning. This forces the LLM to rebuild its refusal mechanisms from within, leading to a more resilient and robust defense against adversarial prompts. The authors evaluate DeepRefusal on several open-source LLMs and against various attack strategies, demonstrating significant reductions in attack success rates while preserving model capabilities.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in the approach of *probabilistic ablation of the refusal direction within the model's representation space during fine-tuning*. Previous methods mostly focused on adversarial training using specific jailbreak prompts or input/output filtering. DeepRefusal attempts a more fundamental approach by directly influencing the internal safety mechanisms. While the concept of refusal direction isn't new (building upon work by Arditi et al.), the method of probabilistically ablating this direction across layers and token depth to force the model to rebuild its safety mechanism is a novel and interesting idea. It moves away from the "arms race" of specific prompt engineering and adversarial examples toward a more general defense mechanism.

*   **Significance:** The significance is potentially high, particularly if the results generalize well to larger, more complex models and a wider range of attack types. The paper identifies a clear weakness in existing safety alignment (alignment depth problem) and proposes a solution that addresses it directly. The experimental results seem promising, with substantial improvements in ASR across various LLMs. Furthermore, demonstrating resilience against refusal direction attacks highlights a critical area often overlooked by state-of-the-art methods. A significant benefit of this approach is that it seems less computationally expensive compared to other techniques like adversarial training on huge jailbreak datasets.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem identification and motivation.
    *   Novel and well-explained method.
    *   Extensive experimental validation on multiple LLMs and attack types.
    *   Ablation studies to demonstrate the contribution of individual components.
    *   Addresses the limitation of safety alignment depth.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The method is fundamentally tied to the concept of "refusal direction". While the paper references works showing its existence in LLMs, the applicability of this method to models where such a direction is not well-defined might be questionable.
    *   The hyperparameter tuning (specifically the PAA probability `p`) seems somewhat ad-hoc. A more principled approach to setting this parameter could further enhance the method's effectiveness.
    *   The paper mentions a lack of careful design for over-refusal mitigation datasets.
    *   The study could benefit from additional experimentation on larger and more challenging datasets and models.
    *   The paper may have missed comparison with recent work targeting the safety problem using contrastive learning.

*   **Potential Influence:** If the approach proves to be generally effective and scalable, DeepRefusal could have a significant influence on how LLMs are aligned for safety. It offers a potential path toward more robust and generalizable defenses against jailbreak attacks, moving away from reactive measures to more proactive and fundamental improvements in model safety. Its ability to work even under extreme conditions, such as the presence of adversarial manipulations, makes it a potential game-changer.

*   **Rigorous rationale**: The paper identifies limitations of current methods, proposing an interesting solution at the representation level and validates this with significant improvement in attack success rate across various architectures and attacks, and addresses ablation studies. Furthermore, it is also shown to generalize well across cross-lingual and random seed attacks. However, there are still several open questions remaining: a dependence on the refusal direction definition, fine tuning of hyper parameters to the specific task, and reliance on high-quality data. Further study is warranted to show generalizability across tasks/architectures, making it difficult to increase the rating beyond 7 at this stage.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Fair-GPTQ: Bias-Aware Quantization for Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15206v1)
- **Authors**: Irina Proskurina, Guillaume Metzler, Julien Velcin
- **Abstract**: High memory demands of generative language models have drawn attention to quantization, which reduces computational cost, memory usage, and latency by mapping model weights to lower-precision integers. Approaches such as GPTQ effectively minimize input-weight product errors during quantization; however, recent empirical studies show that they can increase biased outputs and degrade performance on fairness benchmarks, and it remains unclear which specific weights cause this issue. In this work, we draw new links between quantization and model fairness by adding explicit group-fairness constraints to the quantization objective and introduce Fair-GPTQ, the first quantization method explicitly designed to reduce unfairness in large language models. The added constraints guide the learning of the rounding operation toward less-biased text generation for protected groups. Specifically, we focus on stereotype generation involving occupational bias and discriminatory language spanning gender, race, and religion. Fair-GPTQ has minimal impact on performance, preserving at least 90% of baseline accuracy on zero-shot benchmarks, reduces unfairness relative to a half-precision model, and retains the memory and speed benefits of 4-bit quantization. We also compare the performance of Fair-GPTQ with existing debiasing methods and find that it achieves performance on par with the iterative null-space projection debiasing approach on racial-stereotype benchmarks. Overall, the results validate our theoretical solution to the quantization problem with a group-bias term, highlight its applicability for reducing group bias at quantization time in generative models, and demonstrate that our approach can further be used to analyze channel- and weight-level contributions to fairness during quantization.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Fair-GPTQ: Bias-Aware Quantization for Large Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Fair-GPTQ, a novel quantization method explicitly designed to reduce unfairness in large language models (LLMs) during the quantization process. It addresses the concern that standard quantization techniques like GPTQ, while reducing computational cost and memory usage, can inadvertently amplify biases in LLMs. Fair-GPTQ incorporates group-fairness constraints into the quantization objective, guiding the rounding operation toward less-biased text generation for protected groups (gender, race, religion, occupation). The method modifies the original GPTQ optimization by adding a term that minimizes the difference in model behavior between stereotypical and anti-stereotypical inputs. The authors demonstrate that Fair-GPTQ has minimal impact on overall performance, preserves memory and speed benefits, and reduces unfairness relative to half-precision models, even performing comparably with iterative null-space projection debiasing approaches on racial-stereotype benchmarks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The core novelty lies in the explicit integration of fairness constraints directly into the quantization objective. Prior work has addressed bias in LLMs and quantization independently, but Fair-GPTQ bridges this gap by making bias reduction an inherent part of the compression process. This is a significant step forward and distinguishes it from post-hoc debiasing techniques applied after quantization. The introduction of a pair-difference term to address model bias during quantization is novel.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical and growing concern in the field of LLMs: the amplification of biases during compression.  Quantization is becoming increasingly essential for deploying large models in resource-constrained environments, so a method that can maintain or even improve fairness while quantizing has significant practical implications.  The findings suggest that it's possible to balance the trade-off between model compression and fairness.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition and Solution:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of bias amplification during quantization and offers a well-defined, theoretically grounded solution in Fair-GPTQ.
    *   **Empirical Validation:**  The extensive experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of Fair-GPTQ in reducing bias across multiple benchmarks (CrowS-Pairs, StereoSet, BBQ, SOFA) and for models from the OPT and Mistral families.  The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of different matrix types and layer subsets.
    *   **Comparisons with Existing Methods:**  The comparison with established debiasing techniques (INLP, Self-Debias, SentenceDebias) provides a valuable benchmark for assessing the performance of Fair-GPTQ.
    *   **Efficiency:**  The method maintains the computational complexity of GPTQ, making it a practical solution for large models.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Calibration Data Dependency:** The method relies on the availability of paired stereotypical and anti-stereotypical examples for calibration. While StereoSet is used, this may limit the generalizability of Fair-GPTQ to scenarios where such data is scarce or does not adequately represent the target biases.
    *   **Limited Model Families:** While OPT and Mistral are important model families, validating Fair-GPTQ on a broader range of architectures and pre-training datasets (e.g., Llama, Qwen, more diverse multilingual models) would strengthen the findings.
    *   **Zero-shot trade-off:** As it is a known pattern in many debiasing methods, Fair-GPTQ presents a trade-off between model performance and fairness (zero-shot).
    *  **Limited debiasing attributes:** Most experiments focused on gender, race and religion stereotypes. It would be helpful to also evaluate other sensitive characteristics in bias.

*   **Potential Influence:**  Fair-GPTQ has the potential to influence the development of future quantization techniques and debiasing strategies for LLMs.  It could spur further research into fairness-aware compression methods and contribute to the creation of more responsible and equitable AI systems.  The insights into matrix-level and layer-level contributions to bias could also inform model design and training practices.

*   **Justification for Score:**  While the paper has some limitations regarding the scope of validation and data dependence, its core contribution—integrating fairness directly into quantization—is novel and significant. The empirical results are convincing, and the method is practical. Therefore, a score of 8 reflects the paper's strong contribution to addressing a critical challenge in LLM deployment, while acknowledging the need for further research to enhance its generalizability and robustness.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Geometric Image Synchronization with Deep Watermarking
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15208v1)
- **Authors**: Pierre Fernandez, Tomáš Souček, Nikola Jovanović, Hady Elsahar, Sylvestre-Alvise Rebuffi, Valeriu Lacatusu, Tuan Tran, Alexandre Mourachko
- **Abstract**: Synchronization is the task of estimating and inverting geometric transformations (e.g., crop, rotation) applied to an image. This work introduces SyncSeal, a bespoke watermarking method for robust image synchronization, which can be applied on top of existing watermarking methods to enhance their robustness against geometric transformations. It relies on an embedder network that imperceptibly alters images and an extractor network that predicts the geometric transformation to which the image was subjected. Both networks are end-to-end trained to minimize the error between the predicted and ground-truth parameters of the transformation, combined with a discriminator to maintain high perceptual quality. We experimentally validate our method on a wide variety of geometric and valuemetric transformations, demonstrating its effectiveness in accurately synchronizing images. We further show that our synchronization can effectively upgrade existing watermarking methods to withstand geometric transformations to which they were previously vulnerable.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SyncSeal, a deep learning-based method for geometric image synchronization, designed to enhance the robustness of existing watermarking techniques against geometric transformations like rotation, cropping, perspective changes, and horizontal flips. SyncSeal consists of an embedder and an extractor network. The embedder imperceptibly embeds a synchronization watermark into the image, while the extractor estimates the parameters of the geometric transformations applied to the watermarked image.  Both networks are trained end-to-end using a combination of a synchronization loss (minimizing the error between predicted and ground truth transformation parameters) and an adversarial loss (to maintain high perceptual quality of the watermarked image). The paper experimentally validates the approach on a wide range of geometric and value-metric transformations, and demonstrates that it can effectively improve the robustness of existing watermarking methods against such distortions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of using deep learning for geometric synchronization in the context of watermarking is not entirely new (WMAR uses synchronization layers). However, SyncSeal distinguishes itself by:

    *   **Directly predicting geometric mapping parameters (corner correspondences) instead of relying on template matching or limited transformation types.** This contributes towards greater flexibility and generalizability compared to previous approaches like WMAR.
    *   **Providing a modular synchronization component adaptable to existing watermarking methods.** This allows existing watermarking techniques, that might be vulnerable to geometric transformations, to be upgraded.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper demonstrates empirical results across a broader range of transformations (including perspective transformations) and in conjunction with other watermarking schemes, showcasing clear improvements.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper addresses a practical and well-known limitation of many watermarking schemes: vulnerability to geometric distortions. SyncSeal offers a promising solution to enhance the robustness of watermarking techniques.
    *   The modularity of SyncSeal allows existing watermarking methods to be easily upgraded, thereby having a potential impact on real-world applications.
    *   The approach builds upon recent advances in deep learning-based watermarking and synchronization, demonstrating the feasibility of using such techniques for improving robustness.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   The end-to-end training approach allows the embedder and extractor to be jointly optimized for synchronization accuracy and perceptual quality.
    *   The use of a discriminator helps maintain the imperceptibility of the synchronization watermark.
    *   Experiments are comprehensive, evaluating the approach on a variety of geometric transformations and demonstrating its effectiveness in improving the robustness of other watermarking methods.
    *   The paper is well-written and clearly explains the proposed method and its advantages.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The method doesn't address scale changes (resizing) of the image. While the paper acknowledges this limitation, it is a relevant constraint considering that many real-world scenarios might involve rescaling.
    *   While it outperforms WMAR, there may be other related approaches not thoroughly compared against.
    *   The paper doesn't offer a complete analysis of the trade-offs between watermark capacity (of the primary watermark), synchronization accuracy, and imperceptibility.
    *   The complexity added through the additional embedder and extractor, as well as training, should be fully elaborated.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence the watermarking research community by:

    *   Providing a robust and adaptable solution for geometric synchronization.
    *   Inspiring new research on deep learning-based synchronization techniques for watermarking.
    *   Encouraging the development of more robust and reliable watermarking methods for real-world applications.

*   **Score Rationale:** While the core idea of geometric synchronization isn't completely novel, SyncSeal offers significant improvements in terms of generalizability, modularity, and performance. The comprehensive evaluation and the potential impact on the robustness of watermarking techniques make it a valuable contribution to the field. However, the limitations regarding scale changes and the need for a complete trade-off analysis hold it back from a higher score.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Evil Vizier: Vulnerabilities of LLM-Integrated XR Systems
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15213v1)
- **Authors**: Yicheng Zhang, Zijian Huang, Sophie Chen, Erfan Shayegani, Jiasi Chen, Nael Abu-Ghazaleh
- **Abstract**: Extended reality (XR) applications increasingly integrate Large Language Models (LLMs) to enhance user experience, scene understanding, and even generate executable XR content, and are often called "AI glasses". Despite these potential benefits, the integrated XR-LLM pipeline makes XR applications vulnerable to new forms of attacks. In this paper, we analyze LLM-Integated XR systems in the literature and in practice and categorize them along different dimensions from a systems perspective. Building on this categorization, we identify a common threat model and demonstrate a series of proof-of-concept attacks on multiple XR platforms that employ various LLM models (Meta Quest 3, Meta Ray-Ban, Android, and Microsoft HoloLens 2 running Llama and GPT models). Although these platforms each implement LLM integration differently, they share vulnerabilities where an attacker can modify the public context surrounding a legitimate LLM query, resulting in erroneous visual or auditory feedback to users, thus compromising their safety or privacy, sowing confusion, or other harmful effects. To defend against these threats, we discuss mitigation strategies and best practices for developers, including an initial defense prototype, and call on the community to develop new protection mechanisms to mitigate these risks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Evil Vizier: Vulnerabilities of LLM-Integrated XR Systems":

**Summary:**

The paper explores the novel security risks introduced by integrating Large Language Models (LLMs) into Extended Reality (XR) systems. It identifies vulnerabilities arising from attackers manipulating the context of LLM queries, either through the virtual or real environment, leading to erroneous or malicious outputs that can affect user safety, privacy, and overall experience. The authors categorize LLM-integrated XR systems based on their architecture, inputs, outputs, and triggering mechanisms. They then demonstrate proof-of-concept attacks on various XR platforms (Meta Quest, Ray-Ban Meta, Android, HoloLens) using both open-source and commercial tools, showing how an attacker can subtly influence LLM responses via modified inputs or prompts. Finally, the paper proposes mitigation strategies and provides a defense prototype to protect against malicious code generation, calling for community attention to address these emerging threats.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper addresses a relatively unexplored area at the intersection of LLM security and XR systems. While existing research focuses either on traditional XR security or LLM prompt injection in isolation, this work bridges the gap, highlighting the unique vulnerabilities that arise when the two are combined. The idea of "evil vizier" is a good analogy to conceptualize the threat. It's definitely a novel application of LLM security principles to a new domain.
*   **Significance:** The integration of LLMs into XR is a growing trend, making the security implications of this integration increasingly relevant. The paper demonstrates that seemingly benign manipulations of the environment can have serious consequences, such as misleading users, compromising their safety, or enabling data exfiltration. Identifying and mitigating these risks is crucial for the responsible development and deployment of LLM-enhanced XR applications. The scenarios presented in the proof-of-concept attacks are practical and showcase potential real-world dangers.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Systematic Categorization:** The categorization of LLM-integrated XR systems is helpful for understanding the different dimensions along which these systems can be attacked.
    *   **Practical Attacks:** The proof-of-concept attacks are well-designed and demonstrated on a range of platforms, showcasing the broad applicability of the threat model.
    *   **Clear Threat Model:**  The "evil vizier" threat model is clearly articulated, making it easier for developers to understand the risks.
    *   **Discussion of Mitigation Strategies:**  The paper proposes reasonable and actionable mitigation strategies, even if only at an initial prototype level.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scale of Evaluation:** The study focuses on a limited number of platforms and attack scenarios. While the attacks are effective, a larger-scale evaluation would provide more comprehensive insights.
    *   **Depth of Defenses:** The defense prototypes are preliminary. More sophisticated defense mechanisms would be required to address the full range of attacks.
    *   **Emphasis on Client-Side Attacks:** The study focuses primarily on client-side attacks, neglecting potential vulnerabilities on the server-side (e.g., attacks targeting the LLM itself).

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the development of more secure LLM-integrated XR systems by raising awareness of these new attack vectors and providing a framework for developers to think about security. It will likely inspire further research into more robust defense mechanisms and secure design principles.

**Rigorous Rationale for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and significant contribution by identifying and analyzing security vulnerabilities in LLM-integrated XR systems. The threat model is well-defined, and the proof-of-concept attacks demonstrate the real-world risks. While the scale of evaluation and the depth of defense strategies could be improved, the paper makes a valuable contribution to the field by bringing attention to this emerging area of research and providing a starting point for future work.  The systematic categorization of XR-LLM architectures is valuable for future analysis in this domain.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Assessing Historical Structural Oppression Worldwide via Rule-Guided Prompting of Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15216v1)
- **Authors**: Sreejato Chatterjee, Linh Tran, Quoc Duy Nguyen, Roni Kirson, Drue Hamlin, Harvest Aquino, Hanjia Lyu, Jiebo Luo, Timothy Dye
- **Abstract**: Traditional efforts to measure historical structural oppression struggle with cross-national validity due to the unique, locally specified histories of exclusion, colonization, and social status in each country, and often have relied on structured indices that privilege material resources while overlooking lived, identity-based exclusion. We introduce a novel framework for oppression measurement that leverages Large Language Models (LLMs) to generate context-sensitive scores of lived historical disadvantage across diverse geopolitical settings. Using unstructured self-identified ethnicity utterances from a multilingual COVID-19 global study, we design rule-guided prompting strategies that encourage models to produce interpretable, theoretically grounded estimations of oppression. We systematically evaluate these strategies across multiple state-of-the-art LLMs. Our results demonstrate that LLMs, when guided by explicit rules, can capture nuanced forms of identity-based historical oppression within nations. This approach provides a complementary measurement tool that highlights dimensions of systemic exclusion, offering a scalable, cross-cultural lens for understanding how oppression manifests in data-driven research and public health contexts. To support reproducible evaluation, we release an open-sourced benchmark dataset for assessing LLMs on oppression measurement (https://github.com/chattergpt/llm-oppression-benchmark).
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary**

The paper introduces a novel framework for assessing historical structural oppression worldwide using rule-guided prompting of large language models (LLMs).  The authors address the limitations of traditional oppression measurement methods, which often struggle with cross-national validity due to unique local histories and rely on material resources while overlooking identity-based exclusion. They propose using LLMs to generate context-sensitive scores of lived historical disadvantage based on free-text self-identified ethnicity utterances from a multilingual COVID-19 global study. The authors design rule-guided prompting strategies to encourage the models to produce interpretable, theoretically grounded estimations of oppression. They evaluate these strategies across multiple state-of-the-art LLMs, demonstrating that, when guided by explicit rules, LLMs can capture nuanced forms of identity-based historical oppression within nations. They also release an open-sourced benchmark dataset for assessing LLMs on oppression measurement to support reproducible research.

**Critical Evaluation**

*Novelty:* The paper presents a genuinely novel approach to measuring historical structural oppression. Traditional methods are often limited by relying on pre-defined categories and structured data, which can miss the nuances of individual experiences and cultural contexts. Using LLMs and free-text responses to generate context-sensitive scores is innovative.  The rule-guided prompting strategy to reduce bias and improve interpretability represents a significant advancement.

*Significance:* The paper addresses a critical problem: the difficulty of accurately and comparably measuring oppression across diverse geopolitical settings.  If the proposed method proves robust and scalable, it could have a substantial impact on social science and public health research by providing a complementary measurement tool that highlights dimensions of systemic exclusion.  The open-sourced dataset will also be valuable for other researchers in the field.  The methodology could inform development of more culturally responsive social science research tools. It offers a practical and scalable way to analyze complex social issues.

*Strengths:*
*   The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing methods.
*   The proposed LLM-based approach is innovative and potentially scalable.
*   The rule-guided prompting strategy demonstrates a strong effort to mitigate bias and improve interpretability.
*   The paper includes a thorough evaluation across multiple LLMs and prompting strategies.
*   The release of the dataset promotes reproducibility and further research.
*   The paper addresses an important ethical issue of operationalizing social categories by recognizing the historical, political, and social contingencies of how these categories become meaningful across cultures and contexts.

*Weaknesses:*
*   The study is limited to 334 responses from 10 countries, which might not fully represent the global diversity of identities and contexts.
*   The reliance on LLMs introduces potential biases and limitations associated with these models, as acknowledged by the authors.
*   The transformation of multidimensional oppression into a five-level ordinal scale is a simplification that may obscure certain nuances. The subjectivity of annotations is another limitation.

*Potential Influence:* The paper has the potential to influence the field by introducing a new, scalable method for measuring oppression. It could stimulate further research on using LLMs for social science research and contribute to the development of more culturally sensitive measures of social inequality. It will likely be of interest to researchers in sociology, public health, and computational social science.

*Score Justification:* The paper has several novel aspects. First, it integrates LLMs in an innovative way to address a significant methodological challenge in social science. The application of rule-guided prompting to enhance validity is another notable contribution. These innovations offer a more nuanced way of measuring oppression across different settings. However, some points limit the paper's score. The sample size and limited geographical scope restrict the scope of the analysis. LLMs are prone to error and bias, and the ordinal classification used in the paper necessarily simplifies a multifaceted topic. As a result, while the work offers a useful new approach, it is not without limitations.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Generalizable Geometric Image Caption Synthesis
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15217v1)
- **Authors**: Yue Xin, Wenyuan Wang, Rui Pan, Ruida Wang, Howard Meng, Renjie Pi, Shizhe Diao, Tong Zhang
- **Abstract**: Multimodal large language models have various practical applications that demand strong reasoning abilities. Despite recent advancements, these models still struggle to solve complex geometric problems. A key challenge stems from the lack of high-quality image-text pair datasets for understanding geometric images. Furthermore, most template-based data synthesis pipelines typically fail to generalize to questions beyond their predefined templates. In this paper, we bridge this gap by introducing a complementary process of Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) into the data generation pipeline. By adopting RLVR to refine captions for geometric images synthesized from 50 basic geometric relations and using reward signals derived from mathematical problem-solving tasks, our pipeline successfully captures the key features of geometry problem-solving. This enables better task generalization and yields non-trivial improvements. Furthermore, even in out-of-distribution scenarios, the generated dataset enhances the general reasoning capabilities of multimodal large language models, yielding accuracy improvements of $2.8\%\text{-}4.8\%$ in statistics, arithmetic, algebraic, and numerical tasks with non-geometric input images of MathVista and MathVerse, along with $2.4\%\text{-}3.9\%$ improvements in Art, Design, Tech, and Engineering tasks in MMMU.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**
The paper addresses the lack of high-quality, aligned image-text datasets for geometric reasoning by introducing a novel Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) pipeline. This pipeline synthesizes geometric images from basic relations and uses reward signals derived from mathematical problem-solving to refine captions. The resulting dataset, GeoReasoning-10K, improves cross-modal reasoning in multimodal large language models (MLLMs). The paper demonstrates that models trained on this dataset exhibit better task generalization and achieve accuracy improvements in various mathematical and non-mathematical tasks on benchmarks like MathVista, MathVerse, and MMMU.

**Critical Evaluation:**
*   **Novelty:** The use of RLVR for improving geometric image captioning is a significant and novel contribution. Traditional data synthesis pipelines often rely on templates, limiting generalization. RLVR allows for iterative refinement of captions, ensuring better visual-textual alignment and semantic accuracy. However, the base idea of using RL to improve datasets isn't completely new, but its application to this specific problem and the specific design of the reward function are novel. The specific visual augmentation techniques to enforce semantic relationships in images are also noteworthy.

*   **Significance:** The scarcity of high-quality datasets for cross-modal geometric reasoning is a well-known bottleneck. The paper's creation of GeoReasoning-10K addresses this directly. The demonstrated improvements on multiple benchmark datasets (MathVista, MathVerse, and MMMU) provide strong evidence of the dataset's effectiveness. The finding that training on GeoReasoning-10K improves performance on non-geometric tasks (MMMU) is particularly significant as it suggests improved general reasoning capabilities. However, while the improvements are statistically significant, the absolute gains (e.g., 2.8%-4.8% on MathVista/MathVerse, 2.4%-3.9% on MMMU) are relatively modest, especially given the complexity of the RLVR pipeline. The performance of the base model (Gemma3-4B) and the downstream performance gains on these existing datasets were also not substantially better than those reported in MathVerse.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Well-defined problem and clear motivation.
    *   Novel and well-described RLVR pipeline.
    *   Construction of a new, high-quality dataset.
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation across multiple benchmarks.
    *   Demonstrated improvements in both in-domain and out-of-domain performance.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Modest performance gains compared to the complexity of the pipeline.
    *   Limited model scale. It would be valuable to demonstrate the approach on larger MLLMs.
    *   The gains may be attributed more to improved data quality or the inclusion of the visual augmentations, and less about the design of the specific RLVR loop. Additional ablation studies would clarify.
    *   The base model used isn't cutting-edge.
    *   Quantitative results on existing datasets are just incrementally better that those of MathVerse (see summary above), indicating that the community has already largely solved the problem.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in cross-modal reasoning, dataset synthesis, and the application of RL to data refinement. The GeoReasoning-10K dataset and the RLVR pipeline can serve as a valuable resource for the community. However, the impact is limited given the modesty of the performance gains.

**Score:** 7

**Rationale:**
The paper provides a novel and useful approach to address an important problem, generating cross-modal data for mathematical reasoning. The RLVR data pipeline, the curated dataset, and improved general reasoning abilities of MLLMs are all noteworthy. However, the modest performance gains compared to the increased complexity of the approach limit the impact. A score of 7 reflects a valuable contribution with good novelty, but not a groundbreaking advancement. Moreover, there were already strong datasets in this domain and, as such, the improvements offered here were incremental.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### LNE-Blocking: An Efficient Framework for Contamination Mitigation Evaluation on Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15218v1)
- **Authors**: Ruijie Hou, Yueyang Jiao, Hanxu Hu, Yingming Li, Wai Lam, Huajian Zhang, Hongyuan Lu
- **Abstract**: The problem of data contamination is now almost inevitable during the development of large language models (LLMs), with the training data commonly integrating those evaluation benchmarks even unintentionally. This problem subsequently makes it hard to benchmark LLMs fairly. Instead of constructing contamination-free datasets (quite hard), we propose a novel framework, \textbf{LNE-Blocking}, to restore model performance prior to contamination on potentially leaked datasets. Our framework consists of two components: contamination detection and disruption operation. For the prompt, the framework first uses the contamination detection method, \textbf{LNE}, to assess the extent of contamination in the model. Based on this, it adjusts the intensity of the disruption operation, \textbf{Blocking}, to elicit non-memorized responses from the model. Our framework is the first to efficiently restore the model's greedy decoding performance. This comes with a strong performance on multiple datasets with potential leakage risks, and it consistently achieves stable recovery results across different models and varying levels of data contamination. We release the code at https://github.com/RuijieH/LNE-Blocking to facilitate research.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LNE-Blocking, a novel framework designed to mitigate the impact of data contamination on large language model (LLM) evaluation.  Instead of creating contamination-free datasets, LNE-Blocking aims to restore model performance to a state resembling its capabilities before contamination. The framework decouples the process into two key components: 1) *Contamination Detection*, using Length Normalized Entropy (LNE) to estimate the degree of contamination based on the model's output, and 2) *Disruption Operation*, where a technique called Blocking suppresses the generation of highly probable tokens during decoding to encourage the model to generate non-memorized responses. The intensity of Blocking is adaptively adjusted based on the LNE-detected contamination level.  The authors conduct experiments on code generation and arithmetic reasoning tasks and show that LNE-Blocking effectively restores model performance after contamination, achieving stable recovery across different models and varying contamination levels.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of mitigating contamination *during* evaluation, rather than focusing solely on creating contamination-free datasets or extensive post-hoc sampling, is a valuable contribution. Decoupling the contamination detection and disruption operations offers a modular approach that could potentially be adapted and extended. Using Length Normalized Entropy (LNE) is a reasonable approach to estimate the degree of contamination based on the model's output. The LNE formulation and its adaptive use in adjusting the Blocking intensity show thoughtful design. The `Blocking` mechanism, while simple, provides a means of directly intervening in the decoding process.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical and increasingly relevant problem in the LLM field. Data contamination makes fair benchmarking challenging, impacting the reliability and comparability of model evaluations. A computationally efficient method for *evaluating* potentially contaminated models, especially under greedy decoding, is a useful contribution. Demonstrating stable and robust restoration across tasks and models would have high practical value if the claims hold up under external scrutiny.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Statement:**  The paper clearly articulates the challenge of data contamination and its impact on model evaluation.
    *   **Well-Defined Framework:** The LNE-Blocking framework is well-defined, and its components are logically explained.
    *   **Adaptive Intensity:** Adapting the disruption intensity based on the detected contamination level (using LNE) is a key strength, allowing the method to be more targeted.
    *   **Practical Solution:**  The approach is more practical than building completely new, contamination-free datasets.
    *   **Greedy decoding** Addressing the issue of evaluating models on potentially leaked benchmarks under greedy decoding is useful, as the current work usually requires a considerable amount of time for sampling methods.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope of Evaluation:** The primary evaluation is on code generation and arithmetic reasoning, which are somewhat narrow domains.  More diverse tasks (e.g., natural language understanding, text generation) would strengthen the claims. The performance of LNE-Blocking on smaller, domain-specific model may not be as reliable as in other general LLMs.
    *   **Dependency on a Hyperparameter:** The reliance on the `Threshold_Task` hyperparameter raises a concern about sensitivity to task-specific tuning. A more principled or automated method for setting this parameter would improve the robustness and generalizability of the approach. The selection process and its potential influence on results needed more thorough discussion.
    *   **Oversimplification of Contamination:** Simulating data contamination by continuing pretraining is a common approach, but it's a simplified representation of the complex ways models encounter leaked data.
    *   **Coherence Evaluation**: Coherence metrics should be analyzed further to better understand how the proposed approach affects the natural generation of responses.

*   **Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and useful approach to address a critical problem in LLM evaluation, with promising initial results. The core ideas are well-motivated and the framework is clearly described. However, the relatively limited scope of the evaluation (primarily code and arithmetic), the reliance on a task-specific hyperparameter, and the simplified contamination model restrain the broader applicability and significance of the study at the current stage. Furthermore, the limited diversity on tasks (mostly code generation and math word problems) reduces its generalizability and significance. Overall the paper is a valuable addition to the field, but needs further validation and refinement.

**Score: 6.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Lightweight and Accurate Multi-View Stereo with Confidence-Aware Diffusion Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15220v1)
- **Authors**: Fangjinhua Wang, Qingshan Xu, Yew-Soon Ong, Marc Pollefeys
- **Abstract**: To reconstruct the 3D geometry from calibrated images, learning-based multi-view stereo (MVS) methods typically perform multi-view depth estimation and then fuse depth maps into a mesh or point cloud. To improve the computational efficiency, many methods initialize a coarse depth map and then gradually refine it in higher resolutions. Recently, diffusion models achieve great success in generation tasks. Starting from a random noise, diffusion models gradually recover the sample with an iterative denoising process. In this paper, we propose a novel MVS framework, which introduces diffusion models in MVS. Specifically, we formulate depth refinement as a conditional diffusion process. Considering the discriminative characteristic of depth estimation, we design a condition encoder to guide the diffusion process. To improve efficiency, we propose a novel diffusion network combining lightweight 2D U-Net and convolutional GRU. Moreover, we propose a novel confidence-based sampling strategy to adaptively sample depth hypotheses based on the confidence estimated by diffusion model. Based on our novel MVS framework, we propose two novel MVS methods, DiffMVS and CasDiffMVS. DiffMVS achieves competitive performance with state-of-the-art efficiency in run-time and GPU memory. CasDiffMVS achieves state-of-the-art performance on DTU, Tanks & Temples and ETH3D. Code is available at: https://github.com/cvg/diffmvs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Lightweight and Accurate Multi-View Stereo with Confidence-Aware Diffusion Model":

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a novel multi-view stereo (MVS) framework leveraging conditional diffusion models for depth map refinement.  Instead of relying solely on previous coarse depth estimates, the framework introduces random perturbations inspired by diffusion models to avoid local minima.  Key components include: (1) a condition encoder that fuses matching, image context, and depth context features; (2) a confidence-based sampling strategy that adaptively generates depth hypotheses based on per-pixel predicted confidence; and (3) a lightweight diffusion network consisting of a 2D U-Net combined with convolutional GRUs. The authors present two MVS methods based on the framework: DiffMVS (single-stage refinement) and CasDiffMVS (cascade refinement). Experimental results demonstrate competitive or state-of-the-art performance with improved efficiency in run-time and GPU memory consumption.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the application of diffusion models to the MVS problem, specifically formulating depth refinement as a conditional diffusion process. The combination of a condition encoder, confidence-based sampling, and a lightweight GRU-enhanced U-Net is also a novel contribution.  While diffusion models are increasingly used in various vision tasks, their application to MVS with this specific combination of modules is original.  The confidence-based sampling strategy is another valuable aspect, potentially addressing the limitations of fixed-range sampling in other MVS methods.
* **Significance:** The significance is two-fold. First, the paper addresses the critical challenge of improving both accuracy and efficiency in learning-based MVS. Achieving state-of-the-art (or near state-of-the-art) performance *while simultaneously* reducing computational cost is important for practical applications. Second, introducing diffusion models to MVS opens up new avenues for research and development. The robustness to local minima and the ability to explicitly model depth uncertainty (via confidence) are potentially significant benefits.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Strong Performance:** The reported results show impressive performance on standard MVS benchmarks (DTU, Tanks & Temples, ETH3D), with CasDiffMVS achieving state-of-the-art in several cases.
    * **Efficiency:** The claim of improved efficiency (run-time and memory) is well-supported by the experiments and the design of the lightweight diffusion network.
    * **Well-designed Framework:** The modular design (condition encoder, confidence-based sampling, diffusion network) allows for clear understanding and potential adaptation to other MVS architectures.
    * **Thorough Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of each component of the proposed framework.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Incremental Improvement:** While the results are strong, the core innovation is arguably an incremental adaptation of existing diffusion model techniques to the MVS domain, rather than a completely revolutionary approach.
    * **Complexity:** While the *diffusion network* is lightweight, the overall framework involves multiple modules (feature extraction, cost volume construction, condition encoder, diffusion refinement, upsampling, outlier filtering).  Understanding the precise interplay and importance of each component is complex and requires careful analysis of the ablation studies.
    * **Limited Generalizability Justification:** The authors mention the use of noise scaling strategies to fine-tune the models trained on DTU to the blended MVS. The reasons for this improved performance are not well explained in this paper and needs further exploration.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in MVS, especially regarding the integration of generative models like diffusion models for depth map refinement. The confidence-based sampling and lightweight diffusion network could be valuable building blocks for other MVS architectures. The study could also prompt a more general shift in MVS towards techniques that explicitly model and address depth uncertainty.

**Justification of Score:**

I assign a score of **7.5**.

*   **High Points:** The solid performance, efficiency gains, and potential for future research based on the integration of diffusion models in MVS are substantial. The design and ablation studies are also well-executed.
*   **Lower Points:** The novelty, while significant, is largely an adaptation of existing diffusion techniques. Also, it can be seen that the improvements are very incremental, with only marginal gains over SOTA. While it is clear that improvements have been observed, the precise reasons why improvements occurred remains a mystery to the reader. The explanation provided for some specific adaptations (e.g. The success of using "noise scaling" on DTU to fine-tune model onto blended MVS) is missing.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10
